5. cēviyar es taṉināyakam taṉatu ‘iyaṟkaiyum tamiḻc ceyyuḷum” (1966) eṉṉum nūlil veḷiyiṭṭuḷḷa piṉvarum karuttu mika mukkiyamāṉatu.

“tamiḻc (caṅka) ceyyuḷ tamiḻakattilēyē karukkoṇṭu vaḷarntatu eṉpataṟkāṉa aṉaittuc cāṉṟukaḷaiyum koṇṭuḷḷatu. tamiḻ pēcunar veḷināṭṭiliruntu vantu iṅku kuṭiyēṟiyavarkaḷ eṉṟō, varumpoḻutē vaḷarcciyaṭainta tamiḻ moḻi, ilakkiyattai uṭaṉ koṇṭu vantaṉar eṉṟō kūṟuvataṟkuc caṅka ilakkiyam iṭam taravillai eṉpatē itaṉ poruḷ.”

6. mikap paḻaiya caṅka nūlkaḷilēyē (tolkāppiyam, eṭṭuttokai nūlkaḷil aṭaṅkiya pāṭalkaḷil toṉmai cāṉṟavai) tamiḻ varivaṭivam, eḻuttumuṟai kuṟittum paṇ paṭṭa ilakkiyam kuṟittum teḷivāka, virivākak kūṟap paṭṭuḷḷatu. ai. makātēvaṉ (2003) karuttu, acōkaṉuṭaiya tamiḻ - pirāmi eḻuttukkaḷiliruntu tamiḻ varivaṭivam uruvākkappaṭṭatu eṉpatākum. ikkaruttu ēṟkattakkatāka illai. kāraṇam mēṟcoṉṉa ki.mu. 300 cārnta tamiḻ nūlkaḷ, pāṭalkaḷ uruppeṟuvataṟku cila pala nūṟu āṇṭu kaḷukku muṉṉarē tamiḻ varivaṭivamum ilakkiyaṅkaḷum tōṉṟat toṭaṅkiyirukka vēṇṭum. koṟkaiyil 1970il akaḻnteṭukkappaṭṭa pāṉaiōṭṭil uḷḷa tamiḻ eḻuttiṉ kālam ki.mu. 700 eṉa appoḻutē uṟutiyākkap paṭṭatu. koṭumaṇalil aṇmaiyil akaḻnta pāṉai ōṭṭu eḻuttukkaḷiṉ kālamum ki.mu. 500kku muntiyatāka irukka vēṇṭum eṉa irājaṉ (2004) niṟuviyuḷḷār. āticcanallūril 2004il naṭanta akaḻvāyvil kaṇṭa pāṉai ōṭṭup poṟippiṉ kālamum karimak kaṇippiṉpaṭi ki.mu. 700ai oṭṭiyatāka amaiya lām eṉac ceyti vantuḷḷatu. ivaṟṟaiyum piṟa ātāraṅkaḷaiyum cuṭṭi irājaṉ (2004), naṭaṉakācinātaṉ (2004) ākiyōr “tamiḻukku ki.mu. 800ai oṭṭiyē oru taṉi vari vaṭivam iruntirukkavēṇṭum. ataṉaip piṉpaṟṟiyē acōka tamiḻ - pirāmi varivaṭivam (lipi) ki.mu. 300il uruvākkap paṭṭirukkum” eṉpataṟkāṉa valuvāṉa kāraṇaṅkaḷai muṉ vaittuḷḷaṉar. cintuveḷi nākarika lipi tirāviṭamoḻi cārntatu āṉapaṭiyāl pirāmi lipiyum atiliruntē uruvākiyirukka vēṇṭum eṉpar hīrās (New Review; cūlai 1936). taṉatu paṇṭait tamiḻ eḻuttukkaḷ 1938 nūlil ti.nā. cuppiramaṇiyaṉ kūṟiyataiyum niṉaivu koḷḷa vēṇṭum.

“pirāmi lipi mutalil vaṭamoḻikkāka ēṟpaṭavillaiyeṉṟum uyir eḻuttukkaḷuḷ a, i, u ākiya mūṉṟukkum atikac ciṟappaiyaḷippatum, meyyeḻuttukkaḷuḷ varkka eḻuttukkaḷaik koḷḷātatumāṉa oru pāṣaikkeṉa amaik kappaṭṭup piṉṉāl vaṭamoḻikku upayōkappaṭum paṭi putiya kuṟikaḷ uṇṭākkappaṭṭaṉaveṉṟum eṇṇa vēṇṭiyirukkiṟatu. intiyāvil uḷḷa moḻikaḷuḷ ivvitam uḷḷatu tamiḻ oṉṟutāṉ. aṅṅaṉamāyiṉ pirāmi mutalil tamiḻukkeṉa amainta lipiyāka iruttal kūṭumā? iḵtu ārāy taṟkuriyatu”.

vaṭanāṭṭu varalāṟṟaṟiñar es.ār. kōyal (1979) karuttum acōka pirāmi eḻuttu paṭippaṭiyāka (vaṭanāṭṭil) uruvāṉa talla; atu oru kālakaṭṭattil (ēṟattāḻa ki.mu. 300 eṉak kūṟalām) putitāka uruvākkappaṭṭatu eṉpatākum.

7. tamiḻnāṭṭil irunta ki.mu. 1900 - ki.mu. 300 cārnta peruṅkaṟkāla nākarikattiṉ toṭarcciyē kaḻaka ilakkiya kālam; annākarikak kūṟukaḷiṟ perumpālāṉavai kaḻaka ilakkiyattil kāṇappaṭukiṉṟaṉa eṉpatum rājaṉ pōṉṟavar kaḷuṭaiya aṭutta muṭivākum. āka ivvārāyccikaḷ mēlum muṉṉēṟum poḻutu tolkāppiyattiṉ kālam ki.mu. 300kkum cila nūṟṟāṇṭukaḷ muṟpaṭṭatu eṉa aṉaivarum ēṟṟuk koḷḷum vāyppuḷḷatu.

8. ittaruṇattil iṉṉoṉṟaiyum kūṟa vēṇṭum. ki.mu. 300kku muṟpaṭṭa kaḻaka ilakkiyaṅkaḷ evaiyumē namakkuk kiṭaikkavillai. ki.mu. 300 - ki.pi. 300 kālakaṭṭattaic cārnta caṅka ilakkiyaṅkaḷum muḻumaiyāka namakkuk kiṭaikkavillai. ki.pi. 300ai oṭṭik kaḻaka ilakkiyaṅkaḷait tokuttavarkaḷ evvevaṟṟai viṭṭu vaikkalām eṉṟu karutiṉārkaḷō avaṟṟai maṭṭum tokuttuviṭṭu maṟṟavaṟṟai nīkkiviṭṭaṉar. tokuttavaṟṟilum māṟṟaṅkaḷ ceytirukkalām. nuṇṇaṟi vuṭaiyavarkaḷ itupaṟṟi 1871-liruntu terivittuḷḷavaṟṟaik kāṇpōm:-

cārlas ī kōvar : teṉṉintiya nāṭṭār pāṭal kaḷ (The Folk songs of Southern India) 1871: tiṭṭamiṭṭuc citaikkappaṭāta allatu māṟṟiyeḻutappaṭāta tolpaḻaṅ kālat tamiḻnūl etaiyum iṉṟu kāṇpatu aritu. tamiḻ makkaḷ - tirāviṭa (paḻantamiḻ) ilakkiyattaik kaiviṭṭu purāṇakkataikaḷai nampiṉāltāṉ taṅkaḷukkup piḻaippu uṇṭu eṉṟu uṇarntavarkaḷē ivvāṟu paḻantamiḻ ilakkiyattaic citaittavarkaḷ āvar. (taṉit) tamiḻc ceyyuḷ nūlkaḷ pala matippiḻantu oḻiyumpaṭi avarkaḷ ceytaṉar. aṟavē oḻikka muṭiyāta nērvukaḷilum annūlkaḷai ayōk kiyat taṉamākac citaittu urumāṟṟi ulavaviṭṭu mūlanūlkaḷiṉ karuttai uṇara muṭiyātavāṟu ceytuviṭṭaṉar. civattampi (1986)yum ikkaruttiṉarē.

hayūnevil : ti tapropēṉiyaṉ (The Taprobanian) cūṉ 1896 “maturait tamiḻc caṅkamum ataṉ nūlakamum aḻikkap paṭṭapoḻutu paḻantamiḻ nūlkaḷ cakaṭṭumēṉikku aḻikkap paṭṭaṉa. (avaṟṟuḷ camaṇak koḷkai vāṭai irukkalām eṉṟa karuttil). īḻattil makāvamcam iruntatupōl pāṇṭi yarkaḷ varalāṟṟaik kūṟum oru nūl maturaic caṅka nūlkaḷil oṉṟāka irunta aḻikkappaṭṭu ataṟkup patilāka tiruviḷai yāṭaṟ purāṇam uruvākkappaṭṭu irukka vēṇṭum. aḻikkap paṭṭa varalāṟṟu nūliṉ citaivukaḷuṭaṉ, aiṅkamarkaḷ (pārp paṉiya civa matattavarkaḷ) eṇṇam, camayam ākiyavaṟṟukkup poruttamāṉa puṉai kataikaḷuṭaṉ piṉṉip piṇaintu appurāṇam uruvākkappaṭṭatu”.

jāṉ spiyars “ñāṉam tōṉṟiya iṭattiliruntu” vālyūs Values II - 10 cūlai 1957 : “cintuveḷi nākarika muttiraikaḷil nūṟṟukkaṇakkāṉa (toltamiḻ) vācakaṅkaḷ uḷḷaṉa. kaṭal koṇṭa (aḻintupōṉa) paṇṭai nūlkaḷaip paṟṟit tamiḻ nūlkaḷ kuṟippiṭukiṉṟaṉa. eṉiṉum puttar kālamākiya ki.mu. 600kku muntiya nūlkaḷuḷ oṉṟukūṭa kiṭaik kātataṟkāṉa kāraṇattai nām uṇara muṭikiṟatu. (tol tamiḻaruṭaiya) paṇṭaiya āvaṇaṅkaḷai āriyarkaḷ oṉṟu viṭāmal aḻittuviṭṭaṉar eṉpatē atu”.

te.po. mīṉāṭci cuntaraṉ : tolkāppiyam - collatikāram: kallāṭar urai (1971) : “tol kāppiyattai ataṉ āciriyar enta uruvattil viṭṭuc ceṉṟārō anta vaṭivattil atu namakku vantu cēravillai” (tolkāppiyam : poruḷatikāram marapiyalil pala iṭaic cerukalkaḷ uḷḷaṉa eṉpatu tolkāppiya aṟiñarkaḷ ti. cavarirāyaṉ, kā.cuppiramaṇiyapiḷḷai, cōmacuntara pārati, veḷḷai vāraṇaṉ, ilakkuvaṉār, kamil cuvelapil, irā.iḷaṅkumaraṉ, ce.vai. caṇmukam pōṉṟavar ēṟ
ṟuḷḷatākum. kuṟippāka nālvaruṇam paṟṟik kūṟum marapiyal cūttiraṅkaḷ 71-85 iṭaic cerukal eṉpatu ivvaṟiñarkaḷum (ēṉ naṭunilaiyāḷar aṉaivarum) ēṟpatākum.

9. caṅka ilakkiyac ciṟappiṉ kāraṇamākavē tamiḻaic cemmoḻiyāka ēṟṟuḷḷaṉar. mēlai nāṭukaḷil paṇṭaik kālattilēyē (ippōtaiya airōppiya moḻikaḷil ilakkiyaṅkaḷ evaiyum tōṉṟuvataṟku muṉṉarē) ciṟanta ilakkiyaṅkaḷāka viḷaṅkiya kirēkka, ilattīṉ moḻi ilakkiyaṅkaḷaik “celvilakkiyam” Classical Literature eṉa airōppiyar aḻaittaṉar; avviru moḻikaḷaiyum “cemmoḻikaḷ” Classical languages eṉṟaṉar Classical eṉpataṉ aṭippaṭaip poruḷ “mutal taramāṉa / cevviya / cemmaiyāṉa” eṉpatē yākum. avviru moḻikaḷiṉ (ki.mu. 500 - ki.pi. 300 cārnta) ciṟanta paltuṟai ilakkiyaṅkaḷum antanta moḻiyilēyē tāmāka uruvāṉavai; piṟamoḻit tākkattiṉāl alla. avvilakkiyaṅkaḷum avaṟṟiṉ karuttukkaḷumē piṟṟai airōppiya moḻikaḷukku (āṅkilam, pireñcu, cerumāṉiyam, spāṉiyam, uruciyaṉ mutaliyavaṟṟukku) muṉmātiriyāka amaintaṉa. piṉṉar 18 ām nūṟṟāṇṭiṉ iṟutip pakutiyiliruntu ulakiṉ piṟapakutikaḷil irunta ittakaiya moḻikaḷaiyum cemmoḻikaḷ eṉa airōppiya moḻiyiyalaṟiñarum ilakkiya aṟiñarum ēṟkalāyiṉar;  aḻaikkalāyiṉar. cīṉam, camaṟkirutam, pāracīkam, arapu mutaliyavai 1800 toṭakkattil iruntē ivvāṟu karutappaṭṭu varukiṉṟaṉa. tamiḻ toṉmaiyāṉa moḻi eṉa 1816-liruntutāṉ (pirāṉcis vaiṭ ellis) uṇarappaṭṭatu; 1856-liruntutāṉ (kālṭuvel) ēṟkappaṭṭatu. āṉāl 2000 āṇṭukaṭku muṟpaṭṭa kaḻaka (caṅka) ilakkiyaṅkaḷ, tolkāppiyam, eṭṭuttokai, pattuppāṭṭu, tirukkuṟaḷ, cilappatikāram, maṇimēkalai mutaliyavai – acciṭṭu veḷivanta kālam ki.pi. 1870-1900 āṇṭukaḷēyākum. kirēkka, ilattīṉ, camaṟkiruta tol ilakkiyaṅkaḷukkuc camamāṉa – cila kūṟukaḷil viñciya - paṇṭait tamiḻ ilakkiyam uḷḷatai uṇarntatum tamiḻaic cemmoḻi eṉa mēṉāṭṭaṟiñar 1920kaḷiliruntē naṭaimuṟaiyil ottuk koṇṭuḷḷaṉar. intiya aracu 1800liruntē camaṟkirutam, pāracīkam, arapu ākiyavaṟṟaic cemmoḻikaḷāka “āḷumai naṭaimuṟaippaṭi” (Executive action) ēṟṟukkoṇṭu avaṟṟai aracu nitiyiliruntu celavu ceytu ūkkuvittu vantatu. 2004 aktōparil intiya naṭuvaṇaracu tamiḻaic “cemmoḻi”yāka ēṟpaḷittatu. 2005 cūlaiyiliruntu tamiḻc cemmoḻit tiṭṭattiṉ kīḻp pala naṟpaṇikaḷai maiya araciṉ cemmoḻit tamiḻ niṟuvaṉam ceytu varukiṟatu.

10. cemmoḻit tamiḻ ilakkiyaṅkaḷāṉa caṅka ilakkiyaṅkaḷ paṟṟiya cila virivāṉa ceytikaḷait tamiḻar aṉaivarum aṟintu koḷvatu nalam.

tolkāppiyam

11. tolkāppiyattiṉ kālam ki.mu. 5ām nūṟṟāṇṭu mutal 3ām nūṟṟāṇṭu varai eṉpataip perumpālāṉa naṭunilai aṟiñar ēṟkiṉṟaṉar. camskirutattukku (pāṣaikku) ilakkaṇam varainta pāṇiṉiyiṉ kālam ki.mu. 5ām nūṟ ṟāṇṭu. pāṇiṉikku muntiya camskiruta ilakkaṇaṅkaḷum uṇṭu. vaṭa intiyāviṟkuḷ nākarikamaṟṟa āriyar nuḻainta ki.mu.1500kku muṟpaṭṭa kālattil intiyāveṅkum tamiḻiya moḻikaḷ iruntaṉa. ivaṟṟukku lipiyum iruntatu. ilakkiya vaṭivaṅkaḷum iruntu avaṟṟiṉ vaṭivaṅkaḷum karuttukaḷum rik vētattilum ēṟiyuḷḷaṉa eṉpatu uṇmai. eṉavē tol kāppiyamō allatu ataṟku muntiya tamiḻ ilakkaṇaṅkaḷō (“eṉpa”. “eṉmaṉār pulavar” pōṉṟavai ivaṟṟaic cuṭṭu kiṉṟaṉa) pāṇiṉikku muṟpaṭṭavaiyāka iruntirukkalām eṉac cila tamiḻaṟiñar karutuvatu ātāramaṟṟakūṟṟu alla. “tol kāppiyar” eḻutiyataṉāl “tolkāppiyam” eṉap peyar peṟṟatu eṉpatu paṇṭu toṭṭu varum marapu. āyiṉum tol kāppiyam valla aṟiñarkaḷ, ilakkuvaṉār, ca.vē. cuppira maṇiyaṉ, poṟkō, culevapil pōṉṟavarkaḷ “tol +
kāppu + iyam” = tolkāppiyam, atāvatu toṉ marapaik kākkum nūl eṉak kāraṇap peyar peṟṟatu eṉpatum poruttamākavē uḷḷatu. tolkāppiya eḻuttu, col atikāraṅkaḷilum poruḷ atikāra akattiṇai, puṟattiṇai iyalkaḷilum cila māṟṟaṅkaḷ / iṭaic cerukalkaḷ piṟkālattil ceyyappaṭṭirukkalām eṉpatum poruḷati kārattiṉ iṟuti ēḻu iyalkaḷilum māṟṟaṅkaḷ / iṭaic cerukal mikappala eṉpatum cuvelapil, mār pōṉṟōr karuttu. pirāmaṇiya ātikkam mikunta kālattil tol kāppiyam muḻumaiyāka aḻittoḻikkappaṭāmal pātukāp pataṟkeṉṟē tamiḻ, tamiḻar pāl paṟṟuḷḷavarkaḷum cila iṭaiccerukalkaḷai virakākac ceytirukkalām eṉpar pāvāṇar. (eṉiṉum tolkāppiya mutaluraikārar iḷam pūraṇar kālamākiya ki.pi. 11m nūṟṟāṇṭukku muṉṉarc ceytu muṭikkappaṭṭirukka vēṇṭum) tolkāppiyam “māntaṉ aṟivāṟṟal evvaḷavu viyattaku ucca nilai eytakkūṭum eṉpataik kāṭṭum ciṟanta cāṉṟukaḷuḷ oṉṟu” One of the finest monuments of human intelligence eṉpar cuvelapil. ilakkiyak koḷkaiyil vēṟu entap paṇṭaiya moḻi
kaḷilum illāta ciṟanta karuttukkaḷait tol poruḷ. ceyyuḷiyal kūṟuvatai cuvelapil (1991) kaṭṭurai viḷakku kiṟatu. tolkāppiya eḻuttatikārattil kaṭai āṟu iyal kaḷum puṇarcci paṟṟiyaṉa. “tamiḻil puṇarcci paṟṟiya ilakkaṇam taṉittaṉmaikoṇṭatu. oṭṭunilai viṭṭut taḷarātu, iṉ ṟaḷavum “kaṉṉit tamiḻāka” viḷaṅkuvatu, ippuṇarcci paṟṟiya aṟiviyal aṇuku muṟaikaḷiṉālēyām” (tamiḻaṇṇal, 2004)

12. tolkāppiya ceyyuḷiyal kōṭpāṭṭuc ciṟappu paṟṟi cuvelepil (1974) kūṟiyuḷḷatu varumāṟu:

		tolkāppiyam aṭippaṭai nūlāka iruppatu ilakkaṇa ilakkiya āyvukaḷukku maṭṭumaṉṟu. mānta iṉap paraval cārnta puviyiyal, kumutāya māntaviyal, paṇpāṭu, cūḻnilaiyiyal, uḷaviyal ākiya pulaṅkaḷ cārnta ariya ceytikaḷ annūlil uḷḷaṉa; eṉavē tamiḻar nākarikam- paṇpāṭu cārnta (ēṉ potuvāṉa mānta iṉa nākarikam- paṇpāṭu cārnta) āyvukaḷukkut tolkāppiyam mika mukkiyamāṉatu. eṉiṉum ilakkiyam kūṟuvatellām  (akkālattil nilaviya) uṇmaic camutāya nilaiyai nēraṭiyākak kāṭṭukiṟatu eṉṟu eṇṇutal apattam; uṇmai nilaiyai viruppuveṟuppiṉṟi aṟiviyal pūrvamākak kāṭṭukiṟatu eṉṟu eṇṇutal mēlum apattamākum. akkāla āṇ peṇmakkaḷiṉ akam, puṟa vāḻkkaiyai uḷḷatu uḷḷapaṭi tatrupamāka caṅkap pulavarkaḷuṭaiya pāṭalkaḷ kāṭṭuvatāka camūka varalāṟṟāciriyar cilar muṉṉark koṇṭuḷḷaṉar. āṉāl uṇmai eṉṉa? ceyyuḷ ‘uṇmai vāḻkkai” nikaḻccikaḷai antanta nikaḻcci eppaṭi irukka vēṇṭumeṉṟa ilakkiyamarapu uḷḷatō anta marapukkuk kaṭṭuppaṭṭē cittarikkiṟatu. atāvatu marapukkēṟṟa māṟṟaṅ kaḷuṭaṉ tāṉ cittarikkiṟatu. “uṇmai vāḻkkai” vēṟu; atai ilakkiyattil kāṭṭum marapu vēṟu; eṉpataip pulavarkaḷum poruḷilakkaṇa vāciriyarum uṇarntiruntaṉar. uṇarntiruntataṉāltāṉ uṇmai vāḻkkaiyiṉ kūṟṟukaḷ ceykaikaḷai ‘ulakiyal vaḻakku” eṉṟum ilakkiyattil (ceyyuḷil) vāḻkkaiyaic cittarikkum marapaic ceyyuḷ vaḻakku “pulaṉeṟi vaḻakku” eṉṟum kuṟittaṉar. cila nikaḻccikaḷ, ceytikaḷai ippaṭi ippaṭittāṉ kāṭṭa vēṇṭum eṉṟu ilakkiyappaṭaippāḷarkaḷum vācakarkaḷum tamakkuḷ icaintu uruvākkum mēlvaric caṭṭamākiya marapē ilakkiya marapākum. (e.kā. ovvoru tiṇaikkum uriya mutal, karu, uripporuḷkaḷ). nikaḻcci/ ceyti vācakarukkut terintatu tāṉ; ataṉai oru putiya pārvaiyil, kōṇattil (marapai aṭippaṭaiyil mīṟāmal) kāṭṭum poḻutu vācakar anta nayattai naṉku uṇarntu pārāṭṭa iyalkiṟatu. [itaṉai pi.marutanāyakam tamatu Poetics of the lyric in classical languages” (Chemmozhi I - 2 Jan - March  2007) kaṭṭuraiyil mēlum teḷivākkukiṟār] akappāṭal etuvum orutaṭavai kūṭa talaivaṉ, talaivi peyaraic cuṭṭuvatillai. talaivaṉum talaiviyum eppoḻutum iṉṉār eṉṟu kuṟippāka aṭaiyāḷam kāṭṭa muṭiyātavarkaḷē. eṉavē akap pāṭalkaḷ cittarikkum uṇarvukaḷ (ṭapiḷpu. ec. haṭcaṉ taṉatu “ilakkiyap payiṟcikkāṉa muṉṉurai” 1946 nūlpakkam 97l colvatu pōla “potuvāka enta āṭavaṉukkum peṇṇukkum poruntuvatāka irukkumēyaṉṟi ivaṉukkut tāṉ/ivaḷukkuttāṉ poruntum eṉṟu irukkātu. (uḷḷa uṇarvukaḷai vivarikkātu”) veḷiyulakai vivarikkum puṟappāṭalkaḷil cila potuvāka aṉaivarum nōkkum pārvaiyil irukkum; cila antantap pulavaruṭaiya taṉi nōkkil amaintatāka irukkum. piṉṉavaṟṟukku eṭuttuk kāṭṭu kaiyaṟu nilaippāṭalkaḷ. vaḷḷalkaḷ, naṇparkaḷ iṟantāl pulavarkaḷ tam neñciṉ āḻattiliruntu uṇarvu kaḷaik koṭṭip pāṭum tuyarap pāṭalkaḷ ovvoṉṟum taṉittaṉmai vāyntavai; cātāraṇa kirāmiya oppārikaḷiṉ vālāyap paṭimaṅkaḷaik koṇṭavaiyalla”.

13. tamiḻccemmoḻi ilakkiyattiṉ mukāmai nūl kaḷākiya eṭṭuttokai, pattuppāṭṭu ākiyavaṟṟiṉ (vēṟu entac cemmoḻi ilakkiyattukkum illāta) ciṟappukaḷāka kamil cuvelapil, jārj lūcarṉ hārṭ, ēkē. rāmāṉujaṉ pi. maruta nāyakam ākiyōr terivittuḷḷa karuttukkaḷ cila varumāṟu. (innālvarumē caṅka ilakkiyattai āḻntu payiṉṟavarkaḷ; piṟacemmoḻi ilakkiyaṅkaḷaip paṟṟiyum terintavarkaḷ; nuṇmāṇuḻaipula mikkavarkaḷ):

		“eṭṭuttokai, pattuppāṭṭu nūlkaḷ (mottam 2300 pāṭalkaḷ) acciṭṭu ulakiṉ pārvaikku mutalil vantatu 1880 - 1910 kāla aḷavil ākum. avai veḷivanta akkaṇamē (atuvarai cāmāṉiyap piṟkāla intiya moḻikaḷil oṉṟākak karutappaṭṭatu māṟi ulakiṉ ciṟantu cemmoḻikaḷil oṉṟākat tamiḻ ākiviṭṭatu. pireñcu āciyaviyalaṟiñar piyar meyl “kirēkka uṇarccik kavitaikaḷil talai ciṟanta
navamaṇikaḷukku iṇaiyāṉavai iccaṅka ilakkiyap pāṭal kaḷ; intiyāvil, ēṉ ulakil, ilakkiyap paṭaippukaḷiṉ cikaraṅkaḷil oṉṟu ippāṭalkaḷ” eṉṟu ceytuḷḷa
matippīṭṭai caṅkailakkiyap pāṭalkaḷai āḻntu payiṉṟuḷḷa evarum ēṟṟuk koḷvar.”
- cuvelapil (1960)

		“ulakiṉ nākarikam - paṇpāṭṭukku tirāviṭarkaḷ, kuṟip pākat tamiḻarkaḷ ceytuḷḷa ciṟanta paṅkaḷippukaḷ pallava, cōḻar kāla kōyiṟ kaṭṭaṭakkalai; cōḻar kāla ceppup paṭimaṅkaḷ; parata nāṭṭiyam eṉa aḻaikkappaṭum tamiḻar naṭaṉak kalai; karunāṭaka icai eṉa aḻaikkappaṭum tamiḻ icai mutaliya palavām; avaṟṟuḷḷum talaimai cāṉṟa ciṟappu vāyntatu (caṅka kālac cemmoḻit tamiḻ) ilakkiyam. piṟamoḻi, piṟa nāṭṭu aṟiñar itai uṇarntu ivvilakki yattaip payiṉṟu ulaka mānta iṉattiṉ iṉṟiyamaiyāta uṉṉatamāṉa marapuc celvamāka makiḻntu pōṟṟa vēṇṭum. uccanilaikku muḻut takutiyuṭaiya avvilakkiyattiṟku itu varai annilai tarappaṭavillai.”
- cuvelapil (1973)

“intiyāviṉ piṟa enta moḻi ilakkiyattilum illāta ciṟappuk kūṟukaḷaik koṇṭatu eṉpatu maṭṭu maṉṟip paṇṭait tamiḻ (caṅka) ilakkiyam uṉṉatamāṉa ilakkiya nayamum vāyntatu. ulakiṉ ciṟanta cemmoḻikaḷ varicaiyil tamiḻukkuriya iṭattai caṅka ilakkiyattiṉ 26350 varikaḷ koṇṭa pāṭalkaḷ peṟṟut tantuviṭṭaṉa. av vilakkiyattiṉ ciṟappai ippoḻutu tāṉ ulaku uṇarat toṭaṅkiyuḷḷatu.”

“acai, cīr, taḷai aṭippaṭaiyilāṉa yāppiyal, akam puṟak kōṭpāṭu; uvamaviyal, ākiya aṉaittilum taṉit taṉmai vāynta ciṟanta ilakkiyak koḷkai koṇṭa caṅka nūlkaḷai uruvākkiya perumai tamiḻp paṇpāṭṭukku maṭṭumē uriya ciṟappākum.

“intiyāvil tōṉṟi vaḷarnta cevviyal ilakkiyam - cevviyal moḻip perum paṇpāṭukaḷ iraṇṭu – oṉṟu camaṟkirutam; maṟṟatu tamiḻ; avai oṉṟai oṉṟu cārntavai yalla - cutantaramāṉavai.

“intiya moḻi ilakkiyaṅkaḷilēyē tamiḻilakkiyam tāṉ cevvilakkiyamākavum, nikaḻkāla iyakkamākavum orē nērattil ilaṅkum ciṟappu vāyntatu; atu camaṟkirutac cevviyal ilakkiyam aḷavu toṉmai vāyntatu; paṇṭai kirēkka moḻik kavitaikaḷai eppaṭic cevvilakkiyam eṉa ulaku karutukiṟatō atē taṉmai vāyntatu tamiḻc caṅka ilakkiyam. uyirt tuṭippuḷḷa ilakkiyaṅkaḷ iṉṟum uruvākum moḻiyākavum tamiḻ uḷḷatu.

“tamiḻc cevvilakkiyam (caṅka ilakkiyam) makkaḷaip paṟṟiya ilakkiyam; makkaḷ uruvākkiyatu - āṉāl “nāṭṭup puṟa” ilakkiyamaṉṟu”
- cuvelapil (1973)

“vāḻkkai iṉiyatu; makiḻcci taruvatu. cāvu el lōrukkum varuvatu. ivvulaka vāḻviliruntu viṭutalai peṟa vēṇṭum eṉṟa muyaṟciyellām illai. vaḷḷaṇmai
yum nayaṉmaiyum iyalpāka amaikiṉṟaṉa - āṉmīka nalaṉuk kākavō parikāramākavō alla. iṟantapiṉṉar vīrarum cāṉṟōrum tuṟakkattil nilaiyāka vāḻvar; tīyōr mīḷānirayam eytuvar eṉak karutiṉar. nila ulakil pukaḻai niṟīic ceṉṟavar tuṟakkattil iṉiyavāḻvu eṉṉum vekumati peṟuvar: kātal, puṇarcci, tirumaṇam, makkaṭpēṟu ivai mānta vāḻvil niṟaivu peṟuvataṟkāṉa vaḻimuṟaikaḷ eṉak karutappaṭṭaṉa. vaḷḷaṇmai, vīracceyal, piṟarkkuriyāḷaṉāka vāḻtal - ivaṟṟil oruvaṉ celaviṭṭa nāṭkaḷai niṉaittup pulavarum piṟarum kūṟum pukaḻ moḻikaḷ maṭṭumē oruvaṉ cāvukkup piṉṉar nilainiṟpavaiyākum.”
- cuvelapil (1974)

“ekkālattukkum poruntum aḻakiyal kūṟukaḷ, viḻumaṅkaḷ ākiyavaṟṟai caṅka ilakkiyaṅkaḷ nirampak koṇ ṭavai. iraṇṭāyiram āṇṭukaḷukku muṉṉar yātta avvilakkiyaṅkaḷ piṟkālattil (atāvatu navīṉakālat tiṟaṉāyvāḷar) vakuttuḷḷa ilakkiya nayakkōṭpāṭukaḷiṉ paṭiyum uṉṉatamāka ēṟkappaṭuvaṉavāka uḷḷaṉa.

“caṅka ilakkiyattaip payilum oru camutāyamē (taṉinapar maṭṭumalla) avvilakkiya viḻumaṅkaḷai eyta iyalum vakaiyil, eyta viḻaiyum vakaiyil avvilakkiyam amaintuḷḷatu. eṉavē tāṉ taṟkāla ilakkiyat tiṟaṉāyvāḷarum pōṟṟum vakaiyil ulaka māntar aṉaivarum matikkat takka “eṉṟu muḷa” cirañcīvittaṉmai koṇṭuḷḷatu caṅka ilakkiyam”
- cuvelapil (1974)

“paḻantamiḻ ilakkiyamum paṇpāṭum paṭaitta akam - puṟakkōṭpāṭukaḷ ulakilēyē taṉittaṉmaiyum ciṟappum vāyntavai. cila kūṟukaḷil tamiḻilakkiyattukkum camaṟkiruta ilakkiyattukkum oppumai kāṇappaṭalām. paṭṭāl eṉṉa? camaṟkiruta ilakkiyattiliruntu tāṉ tamiḻ ilakkiyam kaṭaṉ peṟṟirukkum eṉa ēṉ karuta vēṇṭum? (nērmāṟākavum nikaḻntirukkalām aṉṟō?)”
- cuvelapil (1975)

“potuvāṉa ceytiyai, teḷivāka, muḻumaiyāṉa nuṇṇiya pārvaiyil nōkki, eḷiya āṉāl vairam pōṉṟa coṟkaḷil, āḻnta poruḷuṭaṉ taruvatē ciṟanta kavitai eṉat taṟkālattil karutappaṭukiṟatu. veḷippaṭaiyāṉa orē ceytiyait taruvataiviṭa aṭukkaṭukkāṉa pala poruḷait taravallatāṉa coṟceṭṭumikka pāṭalē ciṟanta kavitait taṉmaiyuṭaiyatākak karutappaṭukiṟatu. tolkāppiyam nōkku, payaṉ, eccam (colleccam, kuṟippeccam) uḷḷuṟai, iṟaicci eṉṟu kūṟuvatellām itu tāṉ;
- pi. marutanāyakam (2007)

“mutalāvatāka, tamiḻiṉ toṉmai kuṟippiṭattakkatu. taṟkālattiya piṟa intiya moḻi ilakkiyaṅkaḷukku ōrāyiram āṇṭu muṉṉar uruvāṉa ilakkiyattaik koṇṭatu tamiḻ. tamiḻiṉāti nūl ākiya tolkāppiyattiṉ pakutikaḷ ki.mu. 200ai oṭṭiyavai eṉpatu mikap paḻaiya [tamiḻik] kal veṭṭukaḷiliruntu terikiṟatu. paḻantamiḻp perunūlkaḷāṉa eṭṭuttokai, pattuppāṭṭu mutaliyavai ki.pi oṉṟu - iraṇṭu nūṟṟāṇṭukaḷaic cārntavai. intiyāviṉ miku toṉmai vāynta, camayam cārāta (secular) pāṭalkaḷ avaiyē; kāḷitācaṉ kāviyaṅkaḷukku irunūṟu āṇṭu muṟpaṭṭavai.”

“iraṇṭāvatāka, tamiḻ ilakkiyamarapu maṭṭum tāṉ (camskirutattiliruntu peṟappaṭāta) intiya maṇṇukkuriya ilakkiya marapu ākum. teṉṉāṭṭil camaṟkiruta tākkam valuppaṭuvataṟku muṉṉarē mukiḻttatu atu. camaṟkiruta/piṟa intiya moḻi ilakkiyaṅkaḷiliruntu māṟupaṭṭa taṉmai yuṭaiyatu atu. tamiḻ taṉakkeṉṟu taṉi ilakkiyak koḷkai, ilakkaṇamarapu, murukiyal (aesthetics) uṭaiyatu; ivaṟṟiṉ aṭippaṭaiyil amainta tamiḻc cemmoḻi ilakkiyam vēṟu emmoḻi ilakkiyattukkum illāta taṉittaṉmai vāyntatu (unique). camskirutattilō vēṟu intiya moḻikaḷilō kāṇaviyalāta nayamāṉa uṇarvai (sensibility – intiya maṇṇukkē uriya uṇarvaip paḻantamiḻ ilakkiyattil kāṇalām; tamiḻppulavarkaḷ paṉṉeṭuṅkālam vaḷart teṭutta ceḻumaiyāṉa uṇarvup pārampariyam atu.

“mūṉṟāvatāka, camskirutam - ilattīṉ, cīṉam, pāracīkam, arapu ākiya moḻikaḷiṉ ilakkiyaṅkaḷil ciṟantavaṟṟukku iṇaiyāṉa taram vāyntatu tamiḻc cemmoḻi ilakkiyam. avvilakkiyam nuṇmāṇuḻaipulamikkatu; palvēṟu pāṭuporuḷ koṇṭatu (muṟkāla intiya ilakkiyaṅkaḷil cāmāṉiyarkaḷaip paṟṟiyum niṟaiyap pāṭiyatu tamiḻilakkiyam maṭṭumē); mānta iṉattukkup potumaiyāṉa viḻumiyaṅ kaḷaik kūṟiyatu. eṉavē piṟa cevviyal moḻikaḷiṉ marapu kaḷuṭaṉum ilakkiyaṅkaḷuṭaṉum camamāka niṟkum uraṉ vāyn tatu atu. ulakiṉ talaiciṟanta aṟanūlkaḷil oṉṟu tirukkuṟaḷ. tamiḻccevviyal ilakkiyattil atu pōṉṟu pala tuṟaikaḷilum ciṟanta nūlkaḷ uṇṭu. avvilakkiyam pāṭit tulakkam tarāta mānta iṉa aṉupavam oṉṟum illai.

“iṟutiyākat taṟkāla intiyap paṇpāṭṭaiyum marapaiyum uṇarvataṟkup payilavēṇṭiya ilakkiyaṅkaḷil tamiḻilakkiyam mutaṉmai vāyntatu. camskirutak kāviyaṅ kaḷil teṉṉaka maraput tākkam uḷḷatu kuṟittu nāṉ virivāka eḻutiyuḷḷēṉ. caṅkakālam toṭaṅki tamiḻilil pāṭappaṭṭa ciṟanta intu camaya pakti ilakkiya nūlkaḷ tām taṟpōtaiya intu camayattukku aṭittaḷamāka irukkiṉṟaṉa. tamiḻ pakti ilakkiyak karuttukaḷ pākavata purāṇattilum piṟa (teluṅku/ kaṉṉaṭa/camskiruta) pakti nūlkaḷilum ēṟṟappaṭṭu intiyā muḻuvatum paraviṉa. vētattukkuc camamākak karutappaṭum tamiḻ [tivyapirapanta] pakti ilakkiyap pāṭalkaḷ teṉṉintiyāvil periya vaiṇavakkōvilkaḷil (tiruppati pōṉṟavai) vētattōṭu cērttu ōtappaṭukiṉṟaṉa. taṟkāla intō āriya moḻikaḷukku camskirutamē mūlamoḻi; atupōla taṟkālat tamiḻukkum malaiyāḷattukkum cevviyal tamiḻē mūla moḻiyākum.

		“ulakattiṉ ciṟanta cemmoḻikaḷil tamiḻum oṉṟu eṉpatu ittuṟaiyil valla aṟiñarukku uḷḷaṅkai nellik kaṉi pōlat terinta uṇmaiyākum”
- jārj el. hārṭ (2003)

“iraṇṭāyiram āṇṭukaḷukkum atikamāṉa varalāṟu uṭaiya mikaccila ulaka moḻikaḷil tamiḻ oṉṟu. ulakiṉ pāriya moḻik kuṭumpaṅkaḷ oṉṟil atutāṉ, maiya iṭam vakikkiṉṟatu. tirāviṭa moḻik kuṭumpam cārnta emmoḻi āyvāḷarukkum toṭakkattiliruntē tamiḻ, tamiḻ varalāṟu tamiḻiṉ paṇṭai ilakkaṇam ākiyavaṟait teriya vēṇṭiyatu iṉṟiyamaiyātatu. tamiḻ ilakkiyac celvattai viñcum ilakkiyam ulakil vēṟu emmoḻiyilum illai. eṉavē tamiḻ ilakkiyac ciṟappai ulakeṅkum aṉaivarum terintu koḷḷa vakai ceyyum moḻipeyarppup paṇikaḷ tēvai.”
- ār.ī. āṣar

caṅka kāla akapporuḷ nūlkaḷ

14. caṅka ilakkiya akapporuḷ pāṭalkaḷiṉ taṉic ciṟappai ē.kē. rāmāṉucaṉ (1969) pārāṭṭiyuḷḷatu varumāṟu:-

“toṉmaiyāṉavaiyē eṉiṉum iṉṟum uyirūṭṭam uḷḷavaiyāy ilaṅkum inta (caṅka ilakkiya) akap pāṭal kaḷaip pōṉṟa nayam vāyntavai intiya ilakkiyaṅkaḷ vēṟu evaṟṟilum illai. appāṭalkaḷ kūṟum vāḻkkai neṟiyilum, vivarikkum nikaḻccikaḷilum ciṟanta cemmoḻi ilak kiyattiṉ piṉvarum kūṟukaḷ uḷḷaṉa: kātalōṭu kaṉivum paṇpāṭum; veḷippaṭaik kūṟṟukkaḷōṭu uḷḷuṟai, iṟaicci, aṅkatam ākiyavaiyum; talaivaṉ, talaivi peyar cuṭṭappaṭāviṭiṉum ōviyam pōṉṟa nuṇṇiya varuṇaṉai; aṭikaḷ cila; avai cuṭṭum poruḷō peritu. tamiḻar aṟivuttiṟaṉiṉ mikat toṉmaiyāṉa eṭuttukkāṭṭu iv vakappāṭalkaḷ; atu maṭṭumalla kaṭanta iraṇṭāyiram āṇṭu tamiḻ ilakkiya paṭaippil ivaṟṟai viñcuvaṉa illai.”

15. akap pāṭalkaḷiṉ amaippu paṟṟi cuvelapil (1986) viḷakkamum karuttiṟkoḷḷat takkatu.
	
		“akappāṭal ovvoṉṟum oru kūṟṟu: talaivaṉ, talaivi, tōḻi, cevili mutaliya cilaruḷ oruvar, nāṭaka māntar pōl, kūṟuvatāka atu amaiyum. avarkaḷ oruvaruk koruvarō, taṉakkut tāṉākavō, nilavai nōkkiyō kūṟuvatai vācakarkaḷākiya nām kēṭkiṟōm; avvaḷavē. nāṭakak kāṭci oṉṟai muḻumaiyāka akappāṭal oṉṟil uḷḷa kūṟṟu vivarittuviṭum. ivai nāṭṭuppāṭalkaḷ pōl viraintu pāṭiyavai alla; oru nikaḻcciyaik kaṇṭa allatu eṇṇippārtta oru kaviñar atu paṟṟiya tamatu eṇṇaṅkaḷai nērākavō urumāṟṟiyō veḷippaṭuttiya pāṭalkaḷum alla. ivai aracavaippāṭalkaḷiṉ naḷiṉam uṭaiyavai; “vaikalum eṇtēr ceyyum taccaṉ tiṅkaḷ valitta kāl pōl” nuṇṇiya pulamaittiṟaṉuṭaṉ yākkap paṭṭavai.

		16. mēṟkaṇṭa iruvar kūṟṟukkaḷiṉ āṅkila mūlaṅkaḷ varumāṟu:

		“In their antiquity and in their contemporaniety, there is not much else in any Indian literature equal to these quiet and dramatic Tamil poems. In their values and stances they represent a mature classical poetry : passion is balanced by courtesy, transparency by ironies and nuances of design, impersonality by vivid detail, leanness of line by richness of implication. These poems are not just the earliest evidence of the Tamil genius. The Tamils in all their 2000 years of literary effort wrote nothing better”.
- A.K. Ramanujan.

		An Aham poem is always a “Kurru”, an utterance by one of the limited number of dramatis personae, (hero, heroine, heroine’s girl-friend, foster mother ete). The reader only overhears what the characters say to each other, to themselves, or to the moon. A poem in this tradition implies, evokes, enacts a drama in a monologue. The poems are not the result of rapid composition like oral epics or as lyrical expessions or sublimations of the poet’s own feelings triggered by some romantic event. They are the result of skillful and subtle care, the sophistication of court poetry, “like a chariot wheel made thoughtfully over a month by a carpenter who tosses off eight chariots in a day.” 
- K.V. Zvelebil. 



puṟappāṭalkaḷ : puṟanāṉūṟu

17. puṟanāṉūṟṟil iṉṟu kiṭṭiyuḷḷa 368 pāṭalkaḷiṉ vakaippāṭu varumāṟu : cērar-22; cōḻar-61; pāṇṭiyar-31; kuṟunila maṉṉar -134; piṟa-120. hārṭ (1999) “āriyarukku muṟpaṭṭa teṉṉintiyāvai, ēṉ perumaḷavukku āriyarukku muntiya intiyāvaiyē vivarikkum āvaṇam puṟanāṉūṟu eṉpar” - a testament of pre-Aryan South India and, to a significant extent, of pre Aryan India..” avarum hāṅk haipeṭs-m cērntu 1999-l puṟanāṉūṟu muḻuvataiyum āṅkilattil pāṭalkaḷāka kavinayattuṭaṉ moḻipeyarttuḷḷaṉar:- The four hundred songs of war and wisdom” (Columbia university Press; New york). 

patiṟṟuppattu

18. patiṟṟuppattil mutal pattum iṟutippattum tavira eṭṭuppattukaḷai cārnta 80 pāṭalkaḷē iṉṟu kiṭṭi yuḷḷaṉa. ovvoru pattukkum oru patikam uḷḷatu. avai piṉṉar eḻutic cērkkappaṭṭavai. pāṭalkaḷil kūṟappaṭāta ceytikaḷaiyum toṉmak kataikaḷaiyum patikaṅkaḷ kūṟukiṉṟaṉa. varalāṟṟuc ceytikaḷ pala iruppiṉum cēra maṉṉaraip pukaḻvataṟkeṉṟē eḻutiya ippāṭalkaḷ puṟanāṉūṟṟil uḷḷa ciṟanta kavinayam vāynta pāṭalkaḷiṉ taram uṭaiyaṉavāka illai. patiṟṟuppattu muḻuvataiyum āṅkilattil urai naṭaiyil peyarttuḷḷavar ē.vi.cuppiramaṇiyaṉ (1980).

tirukkuṟaḷ

19. “aṟitōraṟiyāmai kaṇṭaṟṟāl” eṉpatu tiruk kuṟaḷukkum poruntuvatākum. kuṟaḷiṉ ciṟappukkaḷaip pārāṭṭip palamoḻi, pala nāṭṭu aṟiñarkaḷ kūṟiyavaṟṟait tokuttāl atuvē oru periya nūlākiviṭum! ulakap periyārkaḷil oruvarāṉa ālperṭ cuvaiṭcar 1936il veḷiyiṭṭa “intiyac cintaṉaiyum ataṉ vaḷarcciyum” (Indian Thought and its Development; Hodder and Stoughton; London; PP. 272) eṉṉum nūliṉ 200-205 pakkaṅkaḷil intiyat tattuvañāṉikaḷil tiruvaḷḷuvar talaiciṟantu viḷaṅkuvataip pārāṭṭiyuḷḷār. cila pakutikaḷ varumāṟu:

“intiya makkaḷiṭaiyē paṇṭaik kālattiliruntē piṟa uyirkaḷ aṉaittukkum aṉpukāṭṭi utavum aṟakkoḷkai ((the idea of active love) iruntu vantatai, intiyap paṇṭai ilakkiyaṅkaḷil kāṇum kataikaḷil iruntu aṟikiṟōm. attuṭaṉ kuṟippākat tirukkuṟaḷ eṉṉum nūlil kāṇum aṟavuraic ceyyuḷkaḷiliruntum aṟikiṟōm”.

“(ulakil piṟa uyirkaḷiṭattu aṉpu kāṭṭic ceyal paṭuvatai (life Affirmation) avvaḷavāka valiyuṟuttāta maṉu tarma cāttiram pōṉṟavaṟṟiliruntu māṟupaṭum) kuṟaḷil ulakaiyum vāḻkkaiyaiyum veṟuttu otukkum (world and life negation) paravalākak kāṇappaṭuvatu alla. eṅkō ōriru iṭattil vēṇṭumāṉāl irukkalām.

“piramaṇīyam, puttamatam, pakavatkītai pōṉṟavaṟṟaip pōl immai maṟumaip payaṉ karutiyē aṟaneṟiyil niṟṟal vēṇṭum eṉṟa vāṇikak koḷkaiyaik kuṟaḷ valiyuṟuttu vatu illai. nallatu ceyvatē takkatu eṉṟa uṇarviṉā lēyē nallatu (aṟam) ceyya vēṇṭum eṉṟu kuṟaḷ kūṟukiṟatu (kuṟaḷkaḷ 222, 211). pakavatkītaiyō karumam ceytu koṇ ṭiruppatu pirapañcaniyati eṉṟu vaṟaṭṭuttaṉamāka valintu kūṟukiṟatu. āṉāl, eṉṉa viyappu, kuṟaḷō maṉitaṉ uḻaip patum īṭṭuvatum piṟarukkum piṟa uyirkaḷukkum avaṉ naṉmai ceyvataṟkākattāṉ eṉṟu cāṟṟukiṟatu. (kuṟaḷkaḷ 81, 212.) pakavat kītaippaṭi kaṭamai jātikkut takkapaṭi vēṟupaṭum kuṟaḷō makkaḷ aṉaivarum ‘nallavai ceytoḻukak” kūṟukiṟatu.”

“ēṉai intiya tarmacāstirakkārar uḻaippatil, muyaṟci ceyvatil makiḻvōṭu īṭupaṭu eṉṟu kūṟavillai. kuṟaḷkaḷ 619, 630, pōṉṟavaiyē ulakiyalilum, vāḻkkaiyilum, ārvat tuṭaṉ īṭupaṭumpaṭi aṟivuṟuttukiṉṟaṉa.

“(pauttamum kītaiyum pōlap paṟṟiṉmai, veṟup piṉmai, kollāmai, akimcai ākiyavaṟṟaiyum kuṟaḷ valiyuṟuttiṉālum kūṭa atē camayattil) kuṟaḷ atikamāka valiyuṟuttuvatu aṉpum aruḷumē yām (the living ethic of love). kāṇka kuṟaḷkaḷ 72, 78, 79, 103, 226, 241.

“aṟaneṟicār ulaka vāḻkkai ilaṭciyattait tiṟam paṭac cuṭṭukiṟatu kuṟaḷ. maṉitaṉiṉ taṉi vāḻkkai, piṟa uyir kaḷōṭum ulakattōṭum uṟaḻum avaṉ vāḻkkai ākiya vaṟṟil kaikkoḷḷa vēṇṭiya neṟikaḷaik kuṟaḷ viyattaku paṇpuṭaṉum cālpuṭaṉum naṭaimuṟaikkukanta vakaiyil vakut tuḷḷatu. (Characterised by nobility and good sense). ulaka ilakkiyattil vēṟu eṅkum ivvaḷavu ciṟanta oḷimayamāṉa aṟavurai vācakaṅkaḷai vaḻaṅkum nūlaik kāṇa iyalātu.: (kuṟaḷkaḷ 92, 105, 108, 121, 159, 162, 216, 298, 319, 578, 594, 628, 757, 782, 874, 931, 973, 999, 1007, 1024, 1032)”.

“āka kiṟittuva ūḻit toṭakkak kālattilēyē intiya makkaḷiṭaiyē ulakavāḻkkaiyiṭamum uyirkaḷiṭamum paṇpum aṉpum kāṭṭum oru taṉmai iyalpāka iruntu vantuḷḷataik kuṟaḷiliruntu aṟikiṟōm. pirāmaṇiyam, pauttam, pakavatkītai cār intumatam ākiyavaṟṟiṉ kōṭ pāṭukaḷil illātatu atu. āṉāl kīḻcātiyiṉariṭamum, cātāraṇa makkaḷiṭamum tōṉṟi makkaḷōṭu makkaḷāka vāḻnta ciṟanta camayap pōtakarkaḷ mūlamāka attaṉmai metumetuvāka nāḷaṭaivil intu matattil iṭam peṟa lāyiṟṟu”.

20. kirāliṉ tirukkuṟaḷ cerumaṉ moḻipeyarppaip paṭittuviṭṭuttāṉ ālparṭ cuvaiṭcar 1936 mēṟkaṇṭa vāṟu viyantu pārāṭṭiṉār “Free Hindusthan”  eṉṟa peyaril tārakanāt tās (1884-1958) amerikkāvil naṭattiya itaḻukku tālstāyiṭam oru kaṭṭurai kēṭṭār. tācukku 14-12-1908 aṉṟu tālstāy oru kaṭṭurai aṉuppiṉār; “māntar vāḻkkaikkut tēvaiyāṉa orē viti aṉpuṭaimai; aṉ puṭaimaikkuk kaṭṭaḷaikkal tīyavai ceytārkkum iṉṉā ceyyāmaiyēyākum” [Great religious teachers have laid down] one invariable condition of love, namely the enduring of injuries, insults and violence of all kinds without resisting evil by evil. eṉṟu akkaṭṭuraiyil eḻutiyiruntār mēlum taṉ karuttukkāṉa pala ātāraṅkaḷil oṉṟākat tirukkuṟaḷililiruntu

“iṉṉā ceytārkkum...”
“aḵki akaṉṟa......
“piṟarkku iṉṉā......

mutaliya nālaintu kuṟaḷkaḷiṉ āṅkila moḻi peyarppaiyum tantiruntār. mēlum atē kaṭṭuraiyil tālstāy “maṟu piṟavi” uṭpaṭa pala mūṭa nampikkaikaḷai viṭṭuviṭa vēṇṭum eṉṟum eḻutiṉār [“If only people freed themselves from their beliefs in all kinds of Ormuzds, Bhramas and their incarnation as Krishnas and Christs, from belief in paradise and hell, in reincarnations and resurrections, interference of gods in external affairs and infallibility of Veda, Bible, Koran etc”] ‘pirī intustāṉ’” itaḻil vanta ikkaṭṭuraiyaip paṭitta makātmā kānti teṉ āppirikkāviliruntu tālstāykku  1- 10 1909 aṉṟu kaṭitam eḻutiṉār tālstāyiṉ 14 - 12 - 1908 kaṭitattai acciṭṭup parappa aṉumati kēṭṭu. [intiyar palarum maṟupiṟaviyai (reincarnation) nampuvatāl mēṟkaṇṭa vācakattil reincarnation ai nīkkiviṭa tālstāyiṭam aṉumati kēṭṭār kānti; “nīkkavēṇṭām, eṉpatu eṉkaruttu eṉiṉum tāṅkaḷ virumpiṉāl nīkkikkoḷka” eṉṟu tālstāy maṟu moḻiviṭuttār 7-10-1909 aṉṟu] aṉumati vantavuṭaṉ tālstāyiṉ 14-12-1908 kaṭitattai kāntiyaṭikaḷ acciṭṭu āyirakkaṇakkil parappiṉār. icceytiyiṉ virivaip piṉvarum nūlilkāṇalām: 

		Christian Bartolf (Ed) 1997: “Letter to a Hindoo: Tarakanath Das, LeoTolstoy and Mahatma Gandhi” Gandhi – Information Zentrum; Berlin; Selbs tverlag; pp. 80 

21. tirukkuṟaḷ ivvaḷavu ciṟappu vāyntatāka iruppiṉum 19-20ām nūṟṟāṇṭukaḷil ēṟpaṭṭa tamiḻ, tamiḻar maṟumalarccikku muṉṉar entat tamiḻ maṉṉaṉum aracum kuṟaḷaip piṉpaṟṟiyatāka entak kalveṭṭum, ceppēṭum kuṟippiṭṭatillai’ māṟāka vēta neṟi vaḻuvātum “oru kulattukkoru nīti” kūṟum maṉusmruti pōṉṟavaṟṟaip piṉpaṟṟiyum āṇṭatākavē kalveṭṭukaḷ pōṉṟavaṟṟil tamiḻ maṉṉarkaḷ pīṟṟik koṇṭaṉar!

cilappatikāramum maṇimēkalaiyum

22. cilappatikāramum maṇimēkalaiyum cātāraṇa makkaḷaik kāviyat talaiviyarākak koṇṭavai; taṉit taṉmai vāyntavai. ivviru kāviyaṅkaḷiṉ kālam ki.pi. 350- 450 ākalām eṉpar cuvelapil (1995). kāñcipurattil ki.pi. 5m nūṟṟāṇṭil vacitta tikñāṉar camskirutattil niyāyap piravēca eṉṉum aḷavai nūlai eḻutiṉār. annūlukkum maṇimēkalaiyiṉ cila pakutikaḷukkum neruṅkiya oppumai iruppatāl maṇimēkalaiyiṉ kālattai ki.pi 5m nūṟṟāṇ ṭukkup piṉ taḷḷukiṟār vaiyāpuri. āṉāl maṇimēkalaiyai āṅkilattil peyartta ālaṉ tāṉiyalō (Alain Danielou)  ikkaruttai maṟuttu tamiḻar aḷavai nūṟkaruttukkaḷaiyē
maṇimēkalai āciriyarum, tikñāṉarum payaṉpaṭuttiyiruk kalām eṉṟum maṇimēkalai ki.pi. iraṇṭām nūṟṟāṇṭiṉa tāka irukkalām eṉṟum karutukiṟār.

23. caṅka ilakkiyam paṟṟi virivāka ivviyalilum piṟkāla ilakkiyaṅkaḷil ciṟantavai paṟṟic curukkamākap piṉ iyalkaḷilum tantuḷḷataṟkuk kāraṇam uṇṭu. ṣelṭaṉ pōlak (2003) kūṟuvatu pōl oru nāṭṭiṉ (allatu oru nilapparappiṉ) taṉittaṉmaiyaiyē ataṉ moḻiyum ammoḻiyiṉ ilakkiyamumtāṉ nirṇayikkiṉṟaṉa eṉpatē atu. avar kūṟṟu varumāṟu: teṉ āciyā / intiyā / vaṅkāḷam ākiyavai muṟaiyē teṉ āciya / intiya / vaṅkāḷi ilakkiyattai uruvākkiṉa eṉpatu evvaḷavu uṇmaiyō avvaḷavu uṇmai: antanta ilakkiyaṅkaḷē antanta nilappakutikaḷai ākiyavaṟṟai uruvākkiṉa” eṉpatum ākum. oru nilappakutiyiṉ uṇarvukaḷaip paṭampiṭittuk kāṭṭip parappuvataṉ mūlam appakutiyiṉ taṉittaṉmaiyai ilakkiyamē uruvākkukiṟatu; oru nilappakutiyiṉ taṉmai, appakuti makkaḷiṉ nitarcaṉa vāḻkkai ākiyavaiyum appakutiyil uruvākum ilakkiyamum oṉṟukkoṉṟu tākkam viḷaivippavaiyākum.

Literature may have produced Bengal and India and South Asia as much as South Asia and India and Bengal have produced literature; that literary representation can conceptually organise space; and the dissemination of literary texts can turn that space into a lived reality, as much as space and lived realities condition conceptual organization and dissemination. p 27 of Pollock.

24. mēlē uḷḷa pattikaḷil kuṟitta aṟiñarkaḷiṉ tol kāppiyam / caṅka ilakkiyac cevviyal taṉmaicciṟappu paṟṟiya āṅkila mūlaṅkaḷ varumāṟu; piṟamoḻiyāḷarukku ikkaruttuk kaḷai āṅkilattil terivikka vacatiyāka īṇṭu orē iṭattil tarappaṭukiṉṟaṉa:-

As a source book not only of grammatical and literary study but also of human goography, social anthropology, culture, ecology and psychology, Tholkappiam contains very valuable data, and its importance for the study of Tamil culture, and of cultures in general, can hardly be exaggerated. However one must beware of taking the literary conventions as a kind of direct report, or even worse, as an objective explicit and ‘scientific’ treatment of ‘real life’. In some works it was assumed that the bardic court-poetry reflected directly and with utmost fidelity and realism the conduct of men and women of that age in love and war. But the poetry, based on conventions, reflects ‘real life’ only obliquely, since a literary convention was a pattern of fictional (or ‘ideal’) behaviour. The poets and the theoreticians had a clear understanding of the relationship between ‘Real life’ and its reflection in literature, since they clearly differentiated between the two: ulakiyal valakku denoted things said and done in ‘real life’: Ceyyul valakku (or pulaneri valakku) denoted the practice in poetry of reflecting it. A literary convention is an agreement between writer and reader that certain themes will be represented in a certain way. The reader is thus truly able to appreciate the poet’s skill in handling a familiar theme by means of improvisation. The poetry of the ‘interior’ the subjective akam poetry is totally anonymous in the sense that akam poems never mention the hero or heroine by name, not in a single stanza. Hence they “embody what is typically human rather than what is merely individual and particular W.H.Hudson. An introduction to the study of literature, London. 1946.p97). The ‘objective’ external Puram poetry is partly impersonal. partly personal. Thus, e.g. the elegies are highly personal tributes to dead patrons and friends, genuine and spontaneous, expressing intimate and personal grief, “free from any conventional bucolic machinery”
- K.V. Zvelebil (1974) Tamil Literature (in series: A History
of Indian literature) Otto Harrassowitz. Wiesbaden. pp 35-36

“The discovery of this body of roughly 2300 verse (of Ettuthogai and Pathuppattu during 1880-1910) raised the Tamil language at one stroke from an insignificant neo-Indian Language to the standing of one of the great classical languages of the world. Whoever has made a more intimate acquaintance with this ancient poetry will readily subscribe to the assessment of the French Indologist Pierre Meille, when he states that this poetry is comparable with the choicest gems of old Greek lytical poetry, and that this ‘cycle of Sangam poetry’ represents one of the summits of literary creation in India and the whole world’
- K.V.Zvelebil (1960) NEW ORIENT (bi-monthly) 5, 1960pp

		3. reprinted in Tamil Culture x-2: April-June 1963.
		
		“The Dravidians, and in particular, the Tamils have contributed a great deal to the cultural richness of the world: Pallava and Chola temple architecture, Chola bronze sculpture, the dance form known as Bharatanatyam the so called Carnatic system of music. But proba bly the most significant contribution is that of Tamil literature. which still remains to be “discovered” and enjoyed by the non-Tamilians and adopted an an essential and remarkable part of universal heri tage”
- K.V. Zvelebil (1973) The smile of Murugan--on Tamil
literature of South India; Leiden: E.J.Brill. Preface

		“The early Tamil poetry was rather unique not only by virtue of the fact that some of its features were so unlike everything else in India, but by virtue of its literary excellence, those 26350 lines of poetry promote Tamil to the rank of one of the great classical languages of the world--though the world at large only just about begins to realise it.
 
“it is only the Tamil culture that has produced - uniquely so in India, an independent indigenous literary theory of a very high standard, including metrics and prosody, poetics and rhetoric”

		“there exist in India only two great specific and independent classical and historically attested cultures--the Sanskrit culture and the Tamil culture.”

“Tamil literature is the only Indian literature which is both classical and modern; while it shares antiquity with much of Sanskrit literature, and is as classical, in the best sense of the word as for example the ancient Greek poetry, it continues to be vigorously living modern writing of our days.
		
		“Classical Tamil literature is literature is about and of people. but not a Volksliterature”
- K.V. Zvelebil (1973) The smile of Murugan; pp 1, 4

“Life is condidered pleasent and joyful, death an inevitable end. There is no attempt to obtain release from life. Liberality and goodness are indulged in without motives of penance or recompense. About old age thereis a note of nostalgic resignation.

		“Life after death is represented as an abode of permanent happiness for the brave and good. and permanent suffering for the wicked. The happiness of future life is a reward of those who by their bravery and altruism established their glory (pukal) in this world. Love and courtship, marriage and children are considered necessary modes of personal perfection. If at all anything remains after death, it is the glory and honour, the praise due the memory of the days passed in heroism and in the service of fellow men.”
- K.V.Z. (1974) Tamil literature otto Harrassovitz, p.44.

“Ancient Tamil poetry is multivalent: it is so rich and comprehensive that it includes aesthetic structures and values which give high satisfaction to later ages and periods. It is conceived so that rather a community than a single individual could and can realize all its strata and systems. That is why it has survived generational tastes, and has acquired a permanent and universal position”
- K.V.Z (1974) ibid pp 35-36

In the depth and width of erotic experience and in the conceptual framework of the entire phenomenal world, perceived as akam and puram, Tamil culture is unique andoriginal. And as. for the parallels between Tamil and Sanskrit literature-why must it always be presumed that it was the Tamil works that borrowed from Aryan?”
- K.V. Zvelebil (1975) TAMIL LITERATURE (annals of Tamil
literature, abstaining from value judgements) Leiden: E.J.Brill. p107


The ideal modern poem is expected to be objective, precise, organically complex and as well written as prose. Free from clichés an straddled adjectives, it should have the prose virtues of simplicity and hardness in a language charged with meaning to the utmost possible degree. xxxx A poem of indirection, yielding more than one layer of meaning, is a product of excellent craftsmanship. Tolka-- ppiyar’s insistence on the three aspects nokku, payan and eccam, is clearly indicative of his predilection for a poetry that works subtly, indirectly and sugge stively. Nokku demands every syllable, every word and every line to be in harmony with the poem as a whole. By payan is meant the implicit statement on the purpose of an object. Though eccam which is pf two types--colleccam and kurippeccam - interpreted in diverse ways by the commentators, it is used to avoid explicit complete statements and to exploit the suggestive potential of the minimum number of words employed in the poem.

		The concept of poetry as expressed in Tolhappiyar’s Porulathikaram and the practice of Sangam poets as evidenced in the still cherished anthologies would reveal that the ancient Tamils had a poetics of the lyric which Elder Olson was looking for but could not find in the well known languages of the world. (Elder Olson:1962 “Sailing to Byzantium; Prolegomena to a poetics of the lyric” in Willbur Scot (ed) FIVE APPROACHES OF LITERARY CRITICISM; London-Macmillan.)
- P. Marudanayagam; CHEMMOZHI I - 2 Jan-Mar 2007

“First, Tamil is of considerable antiquity. It predates the literature of other modern Indian languages by more than a thousand years. Its oldest work, the Tolkappiyam, contains parts that, judging from the earliest Tamil inscriptions, date back to about 200 BCE. The greatest works of ancient Tamil, the Sangam anthologies and the Pattupattu,
date to the first two centuries of the current era. They are the first great secular body of poetry written in India, predating Kalidasa’s works by two hundred years.

 
“Second, Tamil constitutes the only literary tradition indigenous to India that is not derived from Sanskrit. Indeed, its literature arose before the influence of Sanskrit in the South became strong and so is qualitatively different from anything we have in Sanskrit or other Indian languages. It has its own poetic theory, its own grammatical tradition, its own esthetics, and, above all, a large body of literature that is quite unique. It shows a sort of Indian sensibility that is quite different from anything in Sanskrit or other Indian languages, and it contains its own extremely rich and intellectual tradition.

“Third, the quality of classical Tamil literature is such that it is fit to stand beside the great literatures of Sanskrit, Greek, Latin, Chinese, Persian and Arabic. The subtlety and profundity of its works, their varied scope (Tamil is the only premodern Indian literature to treat the subaltern extensively), and their universality qualify Tamil to stand as one of the great classical traditions and literatures of the world. Everyone knows the Tirukkural, one of the world’s greatest works on ethics; but this is merely one of a myriad of major and extremely varied works that comprise the Tamil classical tradition. There is not a facet of human existence that is not explored and illuminated by this great literature.

“Finally, Tamil is one of the primary independent sources of modern Indian culture and tradition. I have written extensively on the influence of a Southern tradition on the Sanskrit poetic tradition. But equally important, the great sacred works of Tamil Hinduism, beginning with the Sangam Anthologies, have undergirded the development of modern Hinduis. Their ideas were taken into the Bhagavata Purana and other texts (in Telugu and Kannada as well as Sanskrit), whence they spread all over India. Tamil has its own works that are considered to be as sacred as the Vedas and that are recited alongside Vedic mantras in the great Vaishnava temples of South India (such as Tirupati). And just as Sanskrit is the source of the modern Indo- Aryan languages, classical Tamil is the source language of modern Tamil and Malayalam.

“The status of Tamil as one of the great classical languages of the world is something that is patently obvious to any one who knows the subjects” 
- George L. Hart (April 11, 2000)

		“Tamil is one of only a tiny number of languages in the world with a recorded history of more than two millennia. It has a central position in one of the world’s largest language familites, and no matter from where one enters on the study of comparative Dravidian, early acquaintance with Tamil, its history and its early grammars is imperative. It has a literary heritage which is unsurpassed the world over and fully deserving of the efforts of translation to make it widely known”
-R.E. Asher South Indian Horizons: Gros 70th Birth day
							Felicitation Volume 2004.

iyal 5
kaḷappirar kāla tamiḻakam (ki.pi. 300-600)

nam nāṭṭu varalāṟṟilum cari piṟanāṭṭu varalāṟukaḷilum cari cila nilappakutikaḷiṉ cila kāla varalāṟṟu nikaḻccikaḷ kuṟittu ōraḷavukkāvatu teḷivāka virivākak kūṟuvataṟkāṉa cāṉṟukaḷ kūṭak kiṭaippatillai. akkālaṅkaḷaic cilar iruṇṭa kālaṅkaḷ eṉpar. cila eṭuttukkāṭṭukaḷ varumāṟu:

i) ki.mu. 1700kkup piṉ cintuveḷit tol tamiḻa nākarikam ēṉ muṟṟilum aḻintatu? eppaṭi?
ii) cīṉāvil cau paramparaiyiṉar āṇṭa ki.mu. 900- 300 kālattil ciṟṟaracar mikappalar āṇṭaṉar. āṉāl vivaraṅkaḷ teriyavillai.
iii) vaṭaamerikkāvil meccikōvil ki.pi. 3-9 nūṟṟāṇṭukaḷil ciṟantu viḷaṅkiya mayā nākarikam ataṉ piṉ aṭiyōṭu aḻivuṟṟamai.

tamiḻaka varalāṟṟil ki.pi. 300-600 kālattaiyum attakaiya iruṇṭa kālamākap palarum karutukiṉṟaṉar. kaḷappirar kālat tamiḻaka varalāṟṟaip paṟṟi virivāṉa nūl eḻutiyuḷḷavarkaḷ irā. paṉṉīr celvam (1973); mayilai cīṉi vēṅkaṭacāmi (1975); mu. aruṇācalam (1979); naṭaṉakācinātaṉ (1981) ākiyōr āvar.

2. caṅka ilakkiyaṅkaḷ kuṟittuḷḷa pāṇṭiya cōḻa cēra maṉṉaruḷ mikap piṟpaṭṭavarkaḷ kālam ki.pi. 250-300 aḷavil muṭivuṟṟatu. piṟkālap pāṇṭiyar āṭci ki.pi. 560-590il āṇṭa kaṭuṅkōṉ kālam mutal toṭaṅkiyatu. piṟkālac cōḻar āṭci vicayālayaṉ (ki.pi. 848-881) mutal toṭaṅkiyatu. pallava, pāṇṭiya maṉṉarkaḷ āḷukaiyil cōḻanāṭu ki.pi. 848 varait toṭarntatāltāṉ cōḻa paramparai ki.pi. 600 liruntē pāṇṭiyaraip pōla mīṇṭum uyir peṟa iyalavillai.

3. caṅka kālac cēracōḻa pāṇṭiya maṉṉarkaḷ tammiṭaiyē iṭaiviṭātu pōriṭṭu valuviḻanta nilaiyil kaṉṉaṭa nāṭṭavarākiya ciravaṇa paukoḷa vaṭṭārattu kaḷippu nāṭṭuk kaḷappirar (=kaḷappāḷar) ēṟattāḻa ki.pi. 250l allatu ataṟkuc caṟṟup piṉṉar vantēṟikaḷāka vantu tamiḻakattaik kaippaṟṟi pāṇṭiya cōḻa cēra nāṭukaḷai ki.pi. 575 varai āṇṭaṉar eṉpar ma.cī. veṅkaṭacāmi. kē.ār. vēṅkaṭarāmaṉ (1956-57); ka.a. nīlakaṇṭa cāttiri, na. cuppiramaṇiyaṉ, paṉṉīrcelvam, kācinātaṉ, aruṇācalam ākiyōrum ikkaruttiṉarē. pāṇṭiya nāṭu muḻuvataiyumō cōḻanāṭu muḻuvataiyumō kaḷappirar muḻumaiyākat tam āṭcikkuk koṇṭu vantatākak kūṟa iyalātu eṉpar aruṇācalam. kaḷappirar kaṉṉaṭar eṉpataṟkuc cāṉṟukaḷ varumāṟu.

		‘kāṉak kaṭicūḻvaṭukak karunāṭar maṉṉaṉ’
- tiruttoṇṭar purāṇam
mūrtti nāyaṉār.
		“maturai vavviya karunaṭar vēntaṉ”
- kallāṭam

4. pi.ji.el. cuvāmi (1976) karuttu “kaḷappirar paṭai yeṭuppu” eṉpatu ki.pi. 8 ām nūṟṟāṇṭil (kaṉṉaṭiya) kaṅka aracarkaḷuṭaiya orupaṭai tiṇṭukkallukkum maturaikkum iṭaippaṭṭatāṉa pāṇṭiya nāṭṭiṉ oru ciṟu nilap pakutiyaik kaippaṟṟi oru cila āṇṭukaḷ āṇṭataik kuṟippatutāṉ eṉpatākum! akkaruttai aruṇācalam maṟuttuḷḷār. kaḷappirar tamiḻarē eṉṟu karutiya es. kiruṣṇacāmi aiyaṅkār, mu. irākavaiyaṅkār (1930); pi.ṭi. cīṉivāca aiyaṅkār (1929) ka.pa. aṟavāṇaṉ (1974) mutaliyavarkaḷ karuttum iṉṟu ēṟkappaṭuvatillai.

5. kaḷappirar araca kulattavar allar; koḷḷai tēṭi vanta iṉakkuḻuviṉarē (merely a sort of marauding tribe)eṉpar aruṇācalam. kaṉṉaṭa nāṭṭiliruntu vanta kaḷappiraruḷ cāmāṉiyar pēciyatu paḻaṅkaṉṉaṭamāka iruntirukkum (atuvum ki.pi. mūṉṟām nūṟṟāṇṭil irunta - akkālat tamiḻiliruntu atika vēṟupāṭu illāta paḻaṅkaṉṉaṭamāka iruntirukkum). kaḷappiraruḷ āḷum varkkattiṉar camaṇar. camaṇattuṟavikaḷum paṭittavarkaḷum arttamākati moḻiyaip payaṉpaṭuttiya tākat terikiṟatu. eṉavētāṉ ñāṉa campantar tēvārattil (3:39:4)

“manti pōl tirintu āriyattoṭu centamiḻp
payaṉ aṟikilā antakar”

eṉṟu camaṇaraip paḻikkiṟār.

6. tamiḻakattil kaḷappirar kai ōṅki irunta kālattil paḻaiya cēra, cōḻa, pāṇṭiya vēntar paramparaiyiṉar avarkaḷukkuk kīḻ aṭaṅkic ciṟṟaracarkaḷāka iruntirukka vēṇṭum eṉpar vēṅkaṭacāmi. kaḷappira maṉṉarkaḷ peyarkaḷ teriyavillai. ki.pi. 10ām nūṟṟāṇṭu nūlākiya yāpparuṅkala viruttiyurai mēṟkōḷ veṇpā oṉṟu kaḷappāḷaṉ pōr veṟṟiyaip pukaḻkiṟatu. atē virutti  yuraiyāṉatu accuta kaḷappāḷa aracaṉ cēra cōḻa pāṇṭiyavēntarkaḷait taḷaiyiṭṭapōtu avaṉaip pāṭiyaṉavāka nāṉku veṇpākkaḷaiyum tarukiṟatu. kaḷappira maṉṉar aṉaivarum accutaṉ eṉṉum potup peyar tarittaṉar. ki.pi. 5ām nūṟṟāṇṭil cōḻa nāṭṭup pūtamaṅkalattil pautta vikāraiyil taṅkiyirunta poḻutu putta tatta tērar viṉaya viniccayam nūlai eḻutiṉār. annūlum “kaḷappa (kaḷappira) kulattiṟ piṟanta accuta vikkantaṉaik” kuṟikkiṟatu. kaḷappira aracar aṉaivarumē vaiṇavap peyarāṉa accutaṉ eṉṉum ciṟappup peyaraik koṇṭiruntaṉar eṉṟum ma.cī.vē. karutukiṟār. eṉiṉum kaḷappira aracar palar camaṇarākavum iruntuḷḷaṉar.

7. akkālattil ilaṅkaic ciṅkaḷa vēntar aracil nilaiyaṟṟa taṉmai iruntatu eṉpatai cūlavamcam kāṭṭukiṟatu. ki.pi. 436-463 āṇṭukaḷil ilaṅkai aracaip pāṇṭiyar paramparaiyil vanta pāṇṭu (= pāṇṭiyaṉ) 436-441 eṉpavaṉ kaippaṟṟi avaṉum avaṉ makaṉkaḷ iruvarum (441-460) āṇṭaṉar eṉa cūlavamcam kūṟukiṟatu. kaḷappirarukku aṭaṅkiyirunta pāṇṭiya maṉṉar kulattaic cārnta oruvaṉē anta pāṇṭu āka irukkalām eṉpar ma.cī. vē (annūl iyal 3). ki.pi 494-515il āṇṭa ciṅkaḷa maṉṉaṉ mokkallānaṉ I. avaṉukku muṉṉar āṇṭa kassapaṉ I avaṉait turattiya taṉāl mokkallānaṉ tamiḻnāṭṭil aṭaikkalam pukuntiruntatākavum appoḻutu nikantarkaḷ (=camaṇar) uṭaṉ toṭarpu koṇṭu ilaṅkai ceṉṟu kassapaṉai veṉṟa tākavum cūlavamcam kūṟukiṟatu. anta nikantarkaḷ tamiḻakak kaḷappira aracarāka irukka vēṇṭum eṉpar ma.cī.vē.

8. accuta vikkantaṉ kālattil uṟaiyūril iruntu cōḻanāṭṭiṉ oru ciṟupakutiyai āṇṭu vanta cōḻaṉ ceṅkaṇāṉ (ivaṉ puṟanāṉūṟṟil varum ceṅkaṇāṉ allaṉ; pinnāḷil appeyar koṇṭu āṇṭavaṉ “eṇtōḷīcarkku eḻilmāṭam eḻupatu ceytāṉ” eṉpar aruṇācalam.) ivaṉait tirumaṅkaiyāḻvār pārāṭṭukiṟār. kaḷappirar ātikka kālattil cōḻa araca kulattiṉar cilar āntira nāṭṭukkuc ceṉṟu innāḷ kaṭappai karnūl pakutikaḷil “irēṉāṭṭuc cōḻar” eṉṟa peyaril pala nūṟṟāṇṭukaḷ āṇṭu vantaṉar. teluṅkil “cōḻar” eṉpatu “cōṭar” eṉa māṟiviṭṭatu. itu vēṅkaṭacāmi, aruṇācalam iruvar karuttum ākum.

9. cuntarar tiruttoṇṭat tokai yil “kūṟṟaṉ kaḷantaikkōṉ” eṉṟu aḻaikkum maṉṉaṉai nampi yāṇṭār nampi tiruttoṇṭar antāti yil ‘kaḷappāḷaṉ
ākiya kūṟṟuvaṉ’ eṉa aḻaikkiṟār; (kaḷantai iṉṟu tiruttuṟaippūṇṭi arukē uḷḷa kaḷappāl ākum.) vēṅkaṭacāmi pōṉṟōr kūṟṟuvanāyaṉāraik kaḷappāḷar kulattavar eṉṟu colvataṟku ātāramillai. kaḷantaiyai ‘kaḷappu’ eṉat tirintu ‘kaḷappāḷaṉ’ = kaḷantaiyai āṇṭavaṉ eṉṟu uṉṉittuk kūṟiṉar. kūṟṟuvar kaḷappāḷa kulattavar eṉa cuntararum kūṟavillai; nampiyum kūṟavillai. ākavē kūṟṟuva
nāyaṉār kaḷappirar allar, eṉpar aruṇācalam.

1 0 . pi ṟkāla t ti l tamiḻaka va ralāṟṟi l varu m kaḷappāḷarāyar, neṟkuṉṟam kiḻār, accuta kaḷappāḷarāyar (civañāṉapōtam eḻutiya meykaṇṭār tantai) 13ām nūṟṟāṇṭu cārnta cila kaḷappāḷarāyarkaḷ ākiyōrum kaḷappāḷar kulattavar allar. kaḷappirar / kaḷappāḷarai ki.pi. 575kkup piṉ aṭakka utaviya “tamiḻc cātiyiṉarē” kaḷappāḷarāyar eṉappaṭṭavarāvar eṉpatu aruṇācalam karuttu.

11. ki.pi. 575ai oṭṭi kaḷappirarimiṭaruntu pāṇṭiya nāṭṭaip pāṇṭiyaṉ kaṭuṅkōṉ mīṇṭum kaippaṟṟiṉāṉ.

“aḷavariya ātirājarai akalanīkki akaliṭattaik
kaḷapraṉeṉṉuṅ kali araicaṉ kaikkoṇṭataṉai
iṟakkiyapiṉ
.............. kaṭuṅkōṉeṉṉuṅ katirvēṟṟeṉṉaṉ”

- parāntaka neṭuñcaṭaiyaṉ (ki.pi. 756-815) uṭaiya kēḷvikkuṭi ceppēṭu.

“kaḷappāḻaraik kaḷaikaṭṭa maṟṟiraṇṭōḷ
mākkaṭuṅ kōṉ” 		
- parāntaka vīranārāyaṇaṉ (860-905) taḷavāypuram ceppēṭu.

		taṅkaḷaik kaḷappirar tōṟkaṭitta veṟuppil pāṇṭiyar aṉṉāraik kaliyaraicar (tīya aracar) eṉa ikkalveṭṭil paḻittaṉar pōlum; mēlum kaḷappirarukku
camaṇa - puttarkaḷōṭu irunta toṭarpukaḷait tamiḻaka varalāṟṟāciriyar mikaippaṭuttikkūṟukiṉṟaṉar. eṉavētāṉ kaḷappirar “tīya aracar” ākkappaṭṭuviṭṭaṉar eṉpar parṭaṉ sṭeyṉ. camaṇamata untutalāl tāṉ paṇṭait tamiḻ. vēntaraik kaḷappirar veṉṟaṉar; pirāmaṇiya niṟuvaṉaṅkaḷukku ikkaṭṭu ēṟpaṭṭatu eṉṟu nīlakaṇṭa cāttiri kūṟuvataṟku ātāram illai eṉpar sṭeyṉ. ‘All the Kings’s Mana: perspectives on medieval South India’ July 1974 Seminar paper published in J.F. Richard (Kingship and authority in South Asia; University of Wisconsin; Madison 1978.)

ki.pi. 575ai oṭṭi kaḷappirariṭamiruntu cōḻa nāṭṭaip pallava cimmaviṣṇu kaippaṟṟik koṇṭāṉ.

12. piṟkālattil muttaraiyar eṉṟu vaḻaṅkiya kuṟunila maṉṉarum kaḷappirarum potuvāṉa oru kuṭiyiliruntu pirintu vaḷarntavar eṉpār naṭaṉa kācinātaṉ (1976) kaḷappirar camayam

13. kaḷappirar camayam perumpālum camaṇam; cōḻa nāṭṭaik kaippaṟṟiyavarkaḷ maṭṭum pauttam eṉa vēṅkaṭacāmiyum aruṇācalamum kāṭṭum ātāraṅkaḷ maṟukkak kūṭiyavaiyākat tōṉṟavillai. tiruñāṉa campantarum tirunāvukkaracarum - kuṟippāka muṉṉavar – camaṇa pautta camayaṅkaḷait tam tēvārap patikaṅkaḷil kaṭumaiyāṉa vacavu moḻikaḷāl tākkuvatum, camaṇaraik ki.pi. 6 ām nūṟṟāṇṭil kaḻuvēṟṟiya ceytikaḷai cēkkiḻārum piṟarum cātaṉaiyāka makiḻcciyuṭaṉ terivippatum mukkiyamāṉa ātāramākum.

14. parṭaṉ sṭeyṉ, 1978 āyvukkaṭṭuraiyil kūṟum maṟṟoru karuttu; ‘kaḷappirar ātikkam muṭintavuṭaṉ tamiḻnāṭṭil camaṇa matattiṉ katai muṭintu viṭṭatu pōla tamiḻnāṭṭiṉarāṉa varalāṟṟāciriyar cuḷuvākak karuti viṭukiṉṟaṉar, makēntiravarmaṉ pāṇṭiya vēntaṉ ākiya perumaṉṉarkaḷuṭaiya matamāka irunta ataṉai avvaḷavu viraivāka oḻittuk kaṭṭiyirukka muṭiyātu; aracavaikaḷil celvākku pōyviṭṭālum vēṟu pala tuṟaikaḷilum (paṇpāṭu ilakkiyam uṭpaṭa) tamiḻnāṭṭil camaṇac celvākku mēlum cila nūṟṟāṇṭukaḷ valuvāka iruntirukka vēṇṭum... āntira, karunāṭakap pakutikaḷil accelvākku mēlum cila nūṟṟāṇṭukaḷ nīṭittatu’ eṉpatākum.

toṇṭai nāṭṭil pallavar

15. ki.pi. 300-600 kāla aḷavil tamiḻakattil toṇṭai nāṭu pallavarkaḷ āṭciyiṉ kīḻ iruntatu. intak kāla aḷavil avarkaḷ mutalil (āntira cātavākaṉaraippōla) pirākirutattilum piṉṉar camskirutattilum cila ceppēṭukaḷai
veḷiyiṭṭuḷḷaṉar. akkālap pallava aracarkaḷ varalāṟṟai eḻutappōtumāṉa cāṉṟukaḷ acceppēṭukaḷil illai. pallava araca paramparai tōṟṟam kuṟittu vēṟupaṭṭa karuttukaḷ
uḷḷaṉa:

cākar allatu pahlavar - pi.ṭi. cīṉivāca aiyaṅkār intiyar - kē.pi. aiyasvāl, nīlakaṇṭa cāttiri nākar - em. cīṉivāca aiyaṅkār

	āntirap pakutiyiṉar - ār. kōpālaṉ; ci. mīṉāṭci, ṭi.vi. makāliṅkam, oy. cupparāyalu.

	tamiḻar [(kurumpar/tiraiyar (toṇṭaiyar)] - irā. rākavaiyaṅkār, rāca nāyakam, ār. cattiya nātaiyar; eṉ. cuppiramaṇiyaṉ (ivarkaḷ karuttuppaṭi tiraiyar eṉṉum tamiḻa marapiṉar vēṅkaṭattukku appāl āṭci niṟuvi, āntira cātavākaṉarukkuk kīḻ cila kālam āṭci ceytu viṭṭu, piṉṉar mīṇṭum taṅkaḷ toṉmaiyakamāṉa toṇṭai nāṭṭukku vantu viṭṭaṉar eṉpatākum.)

camuttira kuptaṉ taṉatu alakapāt tūṇ kalveṭṭil (ki.pi. 350il) kiruṣṇā āṟṟaṅkaraiyil tāṉ pōril veṉṟa maṉṉarkaḷil oruvaṉāka “kāñci viṣṇu kōpaṉai”k kuṟippiṭukiṟāṉ. pallavar ceppēṭukaḷ kuṟippiṭum pallava maṉṉarkaḷil viṣṇukōpaṉum oruvaṉ. (ki.pi 600-900 kāla aḷavil pallavar āṭci kuṟittu aṭutta iyalil kāṇka.)

kaḷappirar kālattil tamiḻ ilakkiyam, kalaikaḷ

16. kaḷappirar kālattil camaṇamum pauttamum araciṉ ātaravuṭaṉ vaḷarntirukka vēṇṭum. eṉavē ki.pi. āṟām nūṟṟāṇṭil ivviru camayaṅkaḷai oḻikka ñāṉa campantar, nāvukkaracar pōṉṟōr pala koṭumaikaḷaic ceyya vēṇṭiyatāyiṟṟu. kaḷappirar kaṉṉaṭar ākaiyāl avarkaḷ tamiḻ moḻikkum ilakkiyattiṟkum ākkam aḷittatākat teriyavillai; eṉiṉum avarkaḷ āṭcik kālattilum tamiḻar moḻiyaiyum, ilakkiyattaiyum nalla muṟaiyil vaḷart tārkaḷ eṉṟu ma.cī.vē. karutukiṟār. akkālattil eḻunta mutaṉmaiyāṉa tamiḻ nūlkaḷ eṉa avar kūṟuvatu:

ki.pi. nūṟṟāṇṭu
patiṉeṇ kīḻkkaṇakku nūlkaḷil 
(ki.pi. 250kku muṟpaṭṭa tirukkuṟaḷ,
kaḷavaḻi nāṟpatu, mutumoḻik kāñci						3-6
ākiya mūṉṟum nālaṭiyārum tavira

koṅkuvēḷ paicāca moḻiyiliruntu peyarttu eḻutiya
peruṅkatai 										5-6

kāraikkālammaiyār nūlkaḷ 							”

iṟaiyaṉār kaḷaviyal 								”	

muttoḷḷāyiramum tirumantiramum ikkālattaṉavē eṉpar paṇṭārattār. “pārppaṉar tamatu mēmpāṭṭai iḻantaṉar eṉpatāltāṉ (kaḷappirar) kālattai iruṇṭakālam eṉṟu colkiṉṟaṉar” eṉṟu mu.rā. perumāḷ mutaliyār (1985) kaṭṭuraiyil kūṟukiṟār.

17. ki.pi. 600kkup piṉṉar caiva vaiṇava camayaṅkaḷ taḻaitta poḻutu kaḷappirar kālattiya camaṇa, putta camayam cārnta tamiḻ nūlkaḷ pala aḻikkappaṭṭirukka vēṇṭum eṉpar vēṅkaṭacāmi. tiruñāṉa campantar camaṇaruṭaṉ nikaḻttiya aṉalvāta, puṉalvātac ceytikaḷ itaik kuṟippaṉavēyeṉka. ēṟkeṉavē itupaṟṟi cārlas ī.kōvar mutaliyavarkaḷ terivittuḷḷa karuttukkaḷai caṅka ilakkiyam paṟṟiya pakutiyil kaṇṭōm. 

18. kaḷappirar kālattil puṟapporuḷ cārntum akapporuḷ cārntum caṅka kālattiliruntu māṟupaṭṭa putiya karuttukaḷ uruvāyiṉa. pōril pakaivaraik koṉṟu peṟum veṟṟiyai viṭa aimpulaṉkaḷ, kāmam, vekuḷi mutaliya akappakaikaḷai (arukar, puttar pōla) velvatē ciṟanta veṟṟi eṉṟu putuk karuttu camaṇa pautta camayaṅkaḷāl uruvāyiṟṟu. caiva vaiṇavarkaḷō māntar akavoḻuk kattaiviṭa paktarkaḷ ‘nāyaki - nāyaka pāvattil’ kaṭavuḷai vaḻipaṭa mēṟkoḷḷum teyvīkak kātalē ciṟantatu eṉṟa putukkaruttai uruvākkiṉar. itaṟku vakai ceyyavē iṟaiyaṉār akapporuḷ / kaḷaviyal eṉṉum putu ilakkaṇa nūlai eḻutiṉar. akkaḷaviyal paṟṟiyum ataṟku caṅka kālattukkup pintiya iraṇṭām nakkīrar uruvākkiya urai paṟṟiyum kataikaḷ uruvākkappaṭṭaṉa. ikkaḷaviyalum ataṉ uraiyum ipputiya karuttai uruvākkiya piṉṉar ki.pi. 7ām nūṟṟāṇṭil appar, campantar, āḻvārkaḷ pōṉṟōr akapporuḷ tuṟaiyamainta paktippāṭalkaḷai iyaṟṟiṉar. itu ma.cī.vē. muṭivu ākum.

19. kaḷappirar kālattil tamiḻ moḻiyum ilakkiyamum tākkuṇṭaṉa; piṉṉar ki.pi. āṟām nūṟṟāṇṭil caiva camayattiṉ kai ōṅkiya piṉṉarē tamiḻ paḻaiya nilaiyaip peṟṟatu eṉpatu aruṇācalattiṉ nilai ākum! avarukku muṉṉar itu paṟṟi eḻutiya catāciva paṇṭārattār, oḷavai turaicāmippiḷḷai pōṉṟavarkaḷ karuttum ituvē. potuvāka varalāṟu paṟṟiya aṟiñarkaḷiṉ karuttukaḷaik (mēlē iyal 1) kāṇpavarkaḷukku ivviru vakaiyiṉariṉ muraṇpaṭṭa nilai viyappait tarātu. icai, nāṭakam, mutaliyaṉa vāḻntaṉa, vaḷarntaṉa eṉpār vēṅkaṭacāmi; vīḻntaṉa
eṉpār aruṇācalam!

20. kaḷappirar āṭci nallatā, keṭṭatā eṉṟa veṭṭu oṉṟu tuṇṭu iraṇṭākak kūṟavillaiyē eṉak karutalām! ittakaiya cikkalāṉa poruṇmaikaḷil appaṭik kūṟa
iyalātu; kūṟuvatum cariyalla eṉpatē ciṟanta varalāṟṟaṟiñar karuttu ākum. ilaṭcakkaṇakkāṉa āvaṇaṅkaḷum pala āyirakkaṇakkāṉa ātāra nūlkaḷum 1789 pireñcup puraṭci paṟṟi irukkiṉṟaṉa. iruntum appuraṭciyiṉ tōṟṟam, viḷaivukaḷaip paṟṟi iṉṟaiya nilaiyilum kūṭak kaṟārāṉa muṭivukaḷaik kūṟa iyalātu eṉpar varalāṟṟaṟiñar ji.em. ṭirevelyaṉ.

“ittakaiya mika mukkiyamāṉa kaṭuñcikkalāṉa viṣayaṅkaḷaip paṟṟi “itu tāṉ varalāṟṟiṉ tīrppu” eṉṟu entak kālattilum kaṟārākak kūṟiviṭa iyalātu”

“ittakaiya viṣayaṅkaḷil kiṭaittuḷḷa varalāṟṟuc cāṉṟukaḷiṉ aṭippaṭaiyil avaravar tamakkut tāmē cintittu ōraḷavukku oru muṭivukku vara iyalum. appaṭic cintittu muṭivukku vara muyalvatu namatu cintaṉaiyāṟṟalukkuc ciṟanta payiṟciyākum; nam aṉaivarukkum iṉṟiyamaiyāta payiṟciyum kūṭa.”

		“varalāṟṟaip payilvataṉ mūlam mānta iṉattiṉ aṉaittup piracṉaikaḷaiyum akaṉṟa nuṇṇiya pārvaiyuṭaṉum amaitiyākavum cintittu muṭivu ceyyum tiṟaṉum vaḻakkamum namakku varum. paṇṭaiya varalāṟṟuc ceytikaḷai ellāk kōṇaṅkaḷiliruntum amaitiyāka eṇṇip pārttu eṭai pōṭum poḻutu, maṉam namatu aṉṟāṭap piracciṉaikaḷaik kūrnta nuṇṇaṟivuṭaṉ purintu koḷḷum tiṟaṉai eytum. mānta iṉattiṉ iṭiyāppac cikkalkaḷaic ceytittāḷ, tiraippaṭam [ippoḻutu, ivaṟṟaiyum viṭa mika mōcamāṉa tollaik kāṭciyaiyum cērttuk koḷḷalām] ākiya pullaṟivāṇmaimikka ūṭakaṅkaḷ araivēkkāṭṭut taṉamāka (ēṉ 1/100) vēkkāṭṭut taṉamākak kūṭa) “eḷimaippaṭutti” parappurai ceytu takiṭutattam paṇṇuvatiliruntu nammaik kāttuk koḷḷa attiṟaṉ utavum.”

		“On such great and complex issues, there can never be a final verdict of history. But at least it is possible to have an opinion of some value. And the attempt to form such opinion in all the historical light now available, is an education to the mind, the sort of education we all most terribly need.”

		‘But, if we calmly study the past from as many angles as possible, we shall all of us gain in wisdom and understanding. We shall acquire a mentality which, when we return to our own problems, will be less at the mercy of newspapers and films. trying to make us take short cuts to truth and to oversimplify the tangled skein of human affairs.”
- G.M. Trevelyan: An autobiography and other essays.

kiruṣṇacāmi, a. 1990 tamiḻnāṭṭu varalāṟu pallavar,
pāṇṭiyar kālam (ki.pi. 500-900) eṉṉum nūlil
kaḷappirar paṟṟiya pakuti; tamiḻnāṭu aracu tamiḻ vaḷarcci
iyakkakam.

iyal 6
pāṇṭiyar - pallavar āṭci
ki.pi.7-9 nūṟṟāṇṭukaḷ

“kaḷappāḷaraik kaḷaikaṭṭa” (taḷavāypuram ceppēṭu) kaṭuṅkōṉ pāṇṭiya aracai mīṇṭum niṟuvi ki.pi. 570-590 āṇṭukaḷil kōlōcciṉāṉ. ivaṉ vaḻit tōṉṟalkaḷāka ki.pi. 7-9 nūṟṟāṇṭukaḷil āṇṭa pāṇṭiyarkaḷ koṭivaḻi paṟṟiyum āṭciyāṇṭukaḷ paṟṟi aṟiñarkaḷiṭaiyē karuttu vēṟupāṭu uṇṭu. avarkaḷ koṭivaḻiyai kē.vi. irāmaṉ (1977) piṉvarumāṟu tantuḷḷār. (atu paṇṭārattār (1956) koṭivaḻiyiliruntu caṟṟē māṟupaṭukiṟatu.)

kaṭuṅkōṉ (575-600)

māṟavarmaṉ avaṉicūḷāmaṇi (600-620)

ceḻiyaṉ cēntaṉ (620-642)
(ivaṉ tirunelvēli māvaṭṭam malaiyaṭik
kuṟicci kukaik kōyilai amaittavaṉ)

māṟavarmaṉ arikēcari (642-700)
(tiruñāṉacampantar kālam)

kōccaṭaiyaṉ raṇatīraṉ (700-768)
(cuntarar kālam)

māṟavarmaṉ arikēcari rācacimmaṉ I
(nammāḻvār kālam)

	neṭuñcaṭaiyaṉ parāntakaṉ (varakuṇaṉ I) 768-815 (caṅkarāccāriyār kālam) (vēḷvikkuṭi, cīvaramaṅkalam, ciṉṉamaṉūr (ciṟiyatu) ceppēṭukaḷ neṭuñcaṭaiyaṉ kālattavai. neṭuñcaṭaiyaṉ parāntakaṉ vēṟu; varakuṇaṉ I

		vēṟu eṉpār paṇṭārattār. parāntakaṉukkum cirivallapaṉukkum iṭaiyil irācacimmaṉ II, (varakuṇaṉ I eṉa iruvar āṇṭirukka vēṇṭum eṉpār paṇṭārattār.)

srīmāṟaṉ cirivallapaṉ 815-862
(teḷḷāṟu pōril pallavaṉ nantivarmaṉ
III iṭam tōṟṟāṉ.)

varakuṇaṉ II 862-885 				parāntaka vīranārāyaṇaṉ 862-905
(tiruppuṟampayam pōril cōḻaṉ 			taḷavāypuram ceppēṭu
ātittaṉ I  iṭam tōṟṟāṉ.)
									rāca cimmaṉ II - 900-920
							(paṇṭārattār karuttuppaṭi irācacimmaṉ III)

2. mutalām varakuṇaṉiṉ (768-815) taḷavāypuram
ceppēṭṭiliruntu āci nāṭṭut tirumaṅkalattaik kaṭuṅkōṉ
piramatāyamākak koṭuttāṉ eṉṟu aṟikiṟōm. arikēcari
māṟavarmaṉ (642-700) hiraṇayakarpamum tulāpāramum
ceytāṉ. iṟaiyaṉār akapporuḷ uraiyil eṭuttuk
kāṭṭākat tarappaṭṭuḷḷa pāṇṭikkōvai nūliṉ
pāṭṭuṭait talaivaṉ ivaṉē eṉpar. māṟavarmaṉ irāca
cimmaṉ ai cōḻa nāṭṭiṉ perumpakutiyaiyum āṇṭatākat
terikiṟatu. parāntaka neṭuñcaṭaiyaṉ (768-815) uṭaiya
vēḷvikkuṭic ceppēṭṭaip paṟṟi muṉ iyalil kaṇṭōm.
vēlaṅkuṭi eṉṉum ūraip paṇṭaittaṉ paḻampeyar nīkki
srīvaramaṅkalam eṉap peyariṭṭu piramatāya kirāmamākki
cujjāta paṭṭaṉukkut tāṉam koṭuttavaṉum ivaṉē.
ivvāṟu pirāmaṇarkaḷukku piramatāyamāka nilam koṭutta
pāṇṭiyar ceppēṭukaḷiṉ camskirutap pakutikaḷil piṉvarum
vācakaṅkaḷ uḷḷaṉa:

“pirāmaṇarkaḷukkut tāṉam ceytataik kāppataik
kāṭṭilum puṇṇiyattiṟkuk kāraṇamāṉatu vēṟetuvum
ippūmiyil illai. atai apakarippataik kāṭṭilum
pāvattiṟkuk kāraṇamāṉatu ippūvulakil etuvum illai.
		*******

“viṣam eṉṟu collappaṭuvatu viṣam alla.
pirāmaṇaṉ cottuttāṉ viṣam. viṣam oruvaṉaik
kollum. pirāmaṇaṉ cottu (apakarippavaṉuṭaiya) puttira
pauttirarkaḷai aḻikkum.”

“pirāmaṇarkkut tāṉ aḷittatum piṟar aḷittatumāṉa
pūmiyai apakarippavaṉ 60,000 āṇṭukaḷ malappuḻuvākap
piṟantu uḻalvāṉ” 
-ciṉmaiṉūrc ciṟiya ceppēṭu

3. srīmāṟa cirivallapaṉ (815-862) ilaṅkai mītu oru taṭavai paṭai eṭuttu veṉṟāṉ. ivaṉ kālattil vaṭakkil pallavarkaḷuṭaṉ mōti, teḷḷāṟṟil pallava nantivarmaṉ III iṭam tōṟṟāṉ. aṭuttu vanta varakuṇaṉ II (862-885) tiruppuṟampiyattil pallavaṉ aparājitaṉ, kaṅkaṉ pirutivipati I, cōḻaṉ ātittaṉ I (vicayālayaṉ makaṉ) ākiyōriṭam tōṟṟataṉāl cōḻa nāṭṭiṉ teṉpakutiyil atuvarai iruntu vanta pāṇṭiyarāṭci appakutiyil muṭivukku vantatu. avaṉ tampi parāntaka vīranārāyaṇaṉ tantatē taḷavāypuram ceppēṭu. “maturai koṇṭa” cōḻaṉ parāntakaṉ ai (905-955) pāṇṭiyaṉ irācacimmaṉ II (900-920)yai veṉṟu pāṇṭiya nāṭṭaik kaippaṟṟiṉāṉ. irācacimmaṉukku ilaṅkai aracaṉ paṭai aṉuppi utaviyum payaṉ illai. ki.pi. 920liruntu pāṇṭiyanāṭu cōḻaṉ parāntakaṉ I āṭciyiṉ kīḻvantu viṭṭatu. kaṭuṅkōṉ paramparai ittuṭaṉ vīḻnta piṉṉar pāṇṭiya araca paramparai mīṇṭum tuḷirttu valuppeṟṟu 13ām nūṟṟāṇṭil tamiḻakattil perum pakutiyaik kaṭaiciyākak kuṟukiya kālam āṇṭu attuṭaṉ muṭivuṟṟatu (iyal 8)

4. ki.pi. 7ām nūṟṟāṇṭiliruntu caiva nāyaṉmārkaḷ, vaiṣṇava āḻvārkaḷ talaimaiyil pirāmaṇiya matam valuppeṟṟatu. nāyaṉmārkaḷil mutaliṭam vakikkum tiruñāṉacampantar, tirunāvukkarar, cuntarar, māṇikkavācakar ākiyōr ikkāla kaṭṭattaic cārntavarkaḷ. māṟavarmaṉ arikēcari (642-700) kālattil ñāṉacampantaṉ camaṇa muṉivarkaḷai aṉalvātam, puṉalvātam mutaliya “aṟputaṅkaḷ” ceytu veṉṟu avarkaḷaik kaḻuvēṟṟiṉāṉ eṉṟu periya purāṇam mutaliyaṉa kūṟukiṉṟaṉa. ataṉpiṉ pāṇṭiya nāṭṭil perumaḷavil irunta camaṇarkaḷ, avarkaḷ kōyilkaḷ, pāḻikaḷ ākiyavaṟṟaic campantaṉ aḻittataip piṉvarumāṟu periyapurāṇam koṇṭāṭukiṟatu :-

pūḻiyaṉ maturai uḷḷār puṟattuḷār amaṇar cērum
pāḻiyum arukar mēvum paḷḷiyum āṉa ellām
kīḻuṟap paṟittup pōkkik kiḷaroḷit tūymai ceytē”

campantaṉ patikaṅkaḷ 383 uḷḷaṉa, ovvoṉṟilum 11 pāṭalkaḷuṭaṉ. ovvoru patikattilum pattām pāṭalil camaṇa pauttar mītu kaṭumaiyāṉa vacavu moḻikaḷ vīcappaṭukiṉṟaṉa - kāraṇa kāriya viḷakkamō, tarukkavātamō iṉṟi. ivaṟṟai aṟiñar va.cu. ceṅkalvarāyapiḷḷai (1946), tamatutēvāra oḷineṟi: campantar - ārāyccip pakuti pakkaṅkaḷ 189-230 il tokuttu tantuḷḷār. cila coṟṟoṭarkaḷ varumāṟu: “aṟivilāta vaṉ camaṇar”, “kuṇṭarām amaṇar”, “puttar camaṇuraitta poytaṉai uttamam eṉak koḷātu”. vēta neṟiyaic camaṇa pauttar kaṭaippiṭikkātataik kaṭumaiyākat tākkukiṟār.

vēṭṭu vēḷvi ceyyāta amaṇkai (315. 1)
vētaneṟi aṟikilātār (331.10)
vētavēḷviyai nintaṉai ceytuḻal ātamil
amaṇoṭu (366.1)

5. nīlakaṇṭa cāttiri 1929il eḻutiya pāṇṭiyar varalāṟṟukku B.S.O.A.S  (1930-2) itaḻil matippurai eḻutiya jē. cārpeṉṭiyar, cāttiri tamatu nūlil camaṇap paṭukolaiyaip pūci meḻukuvataip piṉvarumāṟu kaṇṭittār. “tamiḻakattil akkālattil perumaḷavukku piṟacamaya veṟuppum piṟacamayattiṉar oṟuppum nikaḻntatai cāttiriyār kaṇṭum kāṇāmalum viṭṭuviṭukiṟār. ki.pi. 7ām nūṟṟāṇṭil pāṇṭiya nāṭṭil camaṇar koṭumaiyāka oṟukkappaṭṭaṉar eṉpatil aiyamillai”. tamatu teṉṉintiya varalāṟu (IV Edn 1976) nūlilum cāttiri camaṇar kaḻuvēṟṟap paṭṭatai nampattakkataṉṟu, puṉaikatai eṉṟu otukkukiṟār. cāttiriyiṉ icceyalai cuvirā jeysvāl (1974)m parṭaṉ sṭeyṉ - um kaṇṭittuḷḷaṉar. sṭeyiṉ tamatu intiya varalāṟu, 1998il camaṇaraik kaḻuvēṟṟiyataṟku campantaṉē kāraṇam eṉṟa ceppiyuḷḷār. (“Sambandhar may even have convinced the king to complete his conversion by impaling the heads of 8000 Jaina teachers, an event long celebrated at the major Siva temple of Madura” p. 134) 8000 camaṇaraik koṉṟatai cēkkiḻār, nampiyāṇṭār nampi, oṭṭakkūttaṉ pōṉṟōr pukaḻntuḷḷaṉar. camaṇaraik kaḻuvēṟṟiyataik kāṭṭum ciṟpaṅkaḷ cila kōyilkaḷil iṉṟum uḷḷatai ma.cī.vē. tamatu camaṇamum tamiḻum, 1954 nūlil kuṟippiṭṭu vēṟu pala ātāraṅkaḷaiyum tantuḷḷār. poruḷātārattil kiraṣām viti kūṟuvatu “nalla nāṇayattuṭaṉ tiruṭṭu nāṇayam puḻakkattil viṭappaṭumpoḻutu tiruṭṭukkācē viraintu paravalākac celāvaṇiyākum; nalla nāṇayam otuṅkiviṭum Bad money drives out good money” eṉpatākum. camūkam – paṇpāṭu ivai paṟṟiya māntaviyal maṟṟum varalāṟṟiliruntu nām “itu pōṉṟa kiraṣaram viti camayaṅkaḷ viṣayattilum (ēṉ camayaṅkaḷukku patiliyākac cila nāṭukaḷil irupatām nūṟ ṟāṇṭil kōlōcciya, iṉṟum kōlōccivarum icaṅkaḷukkum) poruntum eṉṟu aṟiyalām. “tamtam kaṭavuḷē kaṭavuḷ, ēṉaiyōr kaṭavuḷkaḷ picācukaḷ.” tam kaṭavuḷai vaḻipaṭuvōr maṭṭumē nirantara mōṭcam celvar; ēṉaiyōr mīḷā nirayattil erivar” eṉṟa naccuk koḷkai kaḷuṭaṉ, vaṉmuṟaiveṟiyuṭaṉ ceyalpaṭum camayaṅkaḷ attakaiya kōṭpāṭukaḷ aṟṟa camayaṅkaḷai aḻittu oḻittu vantuḷḷatai ulaka varalāṟu kāṭṭukiṟatu.

6. pallava aracaṉ makēntiravarmaṉ I ai caivattiliruntu camaṇa camayattukku matam māṟṟiyavar “maruḷnīkkiyār” eṉṟa peyaril camaṇarāka iruntu piṉṉar tirunāvukkaracar eṉṟa peyaril caivarāka māṟiya nāyaṉār. nāyaṉmār 63 pēruḷ talaimaiyāṉa mūvar mutalikaḷ eḻutiya tirumuṟaip pāṭalkaḷ piṉvaruṉavaṟṟai cōḻa maṉṉaṉ irācarācaṉ I kālattil ki.pi. 10ām nūṟṟāṇṭil nampiyāṇṭār nampi tokuttār:-

							patikaṅkaḷ 		pāṭalkaḷ
tiruñāṉacampantar 			384 			4158
tirunāvukkarar (appar) 		307 			3066
nampiārūrar (cuntarar) 		100 			1026
							791 			8250

ivaṟṟait ‘tēvāram’ eṉa vaḻaṅkum marapu ki.pi. 16ām nūṟṟāṇṭukkup piṉṉartāṉ uruvākiyatu. tamiḻilakkiya varalāṟu (1929) nūlil em.es. pūrṇaliṅkam ‘ñāṉa campantar pāṭalkaḷ (piṟa camayaṅkaḷukku etirāṉa) kaṭumaiyāṉa vacavukaḷaik koṇṭavai; tamatu cātiyiṉar mēmpāṭu, nalam ākiyavaiyē avaṟṟiṉ kuṟikkōḷ. appar pāṭalkaḷō paṇpaṭṭa meyyiyaluṇarvum māntanēyamum koṇṭavai; maṉitarkaḷukkuḷ cāti mutaliya pētam pārāṭṭātavai’ eṉṟu kūṟiyuḷḷār. tamiḻiluḷḷa pakti nūlkaḷil (ēṉ ulaka moḻikaḷ aṉaittilum uḷḷavaṟṟilēyē) mikac ciṟantatu ki.pi. 9ām nūṟṟāṇṭil māṇikkavācakar eḻutiya tiruvācakam ākum. ataṉai 1900il āṅkila ākkam ceyta jārj aklō pōp “āṅkilattil Pilgrim’s progress nūlukku oppāṉatu tiruvācakam; paktiyum teyvīka uṇarvum koṇṭa ñāṉiyiṉ aṉupavattil malarntatu” eṉṟu pārāṭṭiyuḷḷār. āṉmīkattaiyum paktiyaiyum “tattuvañāṉikaḷ kuḻāmiṭa miruntu” potumakkaḷiṭam koṇṭu ceṉṟatu tiruvācakam. upaniṣattukaḷ ‘vāḻum vaḻi a way of life ’ eṉpar irātā kiruṣṇaṉ. tiruvācaka āṅkila moḻi peyarppāḷaruḷ oruvarāṉa ji. vaṉmīkanātaṉ “mānta vāḻvē tiruvācakamtāṉ” Thiruvasagam is the life itself” eṉpār. tamiḻ “aṉpu” camskiruta priyah eṉpataiviṭa nayamāṉa col. pakavat kītai 12.14-20: “sa me priyah” kku “he is dear to me, who” eṉpatutāṉ cariyāṉa āṅkila ākkam. āyiṉum Zaehner mutaliyavarkaḷ ‘I love the man who...” eṉa moḻipeyarttuḷḷār. māntaṉukkum kaṭavuḷukkum iṭaiyiluḷḷa uṟavaik kuṟikkum “aṉpu” pōṉṟa arumaiyāṉa col kītaiyil illai eṉpar ē.kē. rāmāṉucaṉ (Hymns for the drowning 1981: nammāḻvār pāṭalkaḷil 85ṉ nayamāṉa āṅkilap peyarppu) Nowhere does anything corresponding to the Tamil anpu (“love”) appear in the Sanskrit Gita as a possible relation between man and God.

7. pakti iyakkam paṟṟiya cempakalaṭcumiyiṉ 1996 kaṭṭuraik karuttukkaḷ kuṟippiṭattakkaṉa. ki.pi. 600kku muṉṉar tamiḻaka aracaravaikaḷilum nakaraṅkaḷilum (e.kā. kāñci, maturai) camaṇa pautta camayaṅkaḷiṉ kai ōṅki iruntatu. accamayaṅkaḷai etirttu caiva vaiṇava camayaṅkaḷ tam kaṭavuḷar pāl paktiyaip parappip pōrāṭiya cūḻnilaiyil eḻuntavai paktippāṭalkaḷ. puṟac camayaṅkaḷ iraṇṭum vīṭupēṟṟukkup piṟappum cātiyum taṭaiyalla eṉṟavai; avaṟṟiṉ kōṭpāṭṭait taḻuvi cāti, varuṇa vēṟupāṭiṉṟi aṉaivarum vīṭu pēṟu aṭaiyalām eṉṟu pakti iyakkamum vaḻivakuttamai appuṟaccamayaṅkaḷai vīḻtta utaviyirukkalām. (The hymnists were propagating bhakti in a situation of conflict and rivalry with non-orthodex sects like the Jains and Buddhists whose influence and dominance in royal and urban centres had been established in the pre 7th centruy period. xxxxx Presumbaly, bhakti, by throwing open the path of salvation to all irrespective of caste and social hierarchy, imbibed the ideals of the non-orthodox creeds, namely birth and caste as no obstacles to salvation, and thereby succeeded in rooting out ‘herctical’ sects.) 

8. itē kālakaṭṭattil malarntavaiyē vaiṇava pakti ilakkiyamum ākum. āḻvārkaḷ 12 pēril perumpālōr ikkālattil vāḻntavarkaḷ - iṉṟaiya tūttukkuṭi māvaṭṭam kurukūril 8ām nūṟṟāṇṭil piṟanta māṟaṉ eṉṉum nammāḻvār (880-930) uṭpaṭa. nālāyira tivya pirapantattil uḷḷa 4000 pāṭalkaḷil nammāḻvār pāṭiya tiruvāymoḻi 1120 pāṭalkaḷum, tirumaṅkaiyāḻvār pāṭiya 1110 pāṭalkaḷum aṭaṅkum. rāmāṉucaṉ tamatu 1981 nūlil pakti ilakkiyattiṉ piṉpulamāka piṉvarum kūṟukaḷ amaintaṉa eṉkiṟār

vēta, upaniṣatak karuttakaḷ
ii. putta, camaṇak karuttukaḷ (caraṇam - pirapatti; caṅkam – 
aṭiyār kuḻām; kuru - cīṭar nerukkam)
purāṇak kaṟpaṉaikaḷ
tamiḻ akapporuḷ, puṟapporuḷ marapukaḷ
pakavatkītai, paripāṭal, tirumurukāṟṟuppaṭai 
mutaliyavaṟṟiṉ karuttukaḷ
nāṭṭār (folk) camayam / meṭṭukaḷ / kaṟpaṉaikaḷ /
karuttukaḷ.

āḻvārkaḷ pāṭalkaḷai aṭittaḷamākak koṇṭu 10ām nūṟṟāṇṭu nātamuṉi, avarpēraṉ yamuṉāccāriyār (10-11 nūṟṟāṇṭu) irāmāṉujācāriyār (1050-1137) ākiyōr uruvākkiyatu viciṣṭātvaitam (qualified monism) eṉṉum vaiṇavak koḷkai. caṅkarar uṭaiya varaṭṭu atvaitam strict monism koḷkaiyaiviṭa atu nayamāṉatu. kaṭavuḷākiya nārāyaṇaṉiṭam pakti celuttuvatu upaniṣattukkaḷiṉ vētāntattukku etirāṉatu aṉṟu eṉpatai irāmāṉujar niṟuviṉār. vaiṇava īṭukaḷ (āḻvār pācuraṅkaḷukku virivuraikaḷ) maṇippiravāḷa naṭaiyil iruppiṉum ciṟanta meyyiyal nūlkaḷākum.

9. caṅka ilakkiya iṟutik kālattilēyē paktip pāṭalkaḷum tōṉṟiviṭṭaṉa (paripāṭal; tirumurukāṟṟup paṭai ) aṭuttu kaḷappirar kālattil 4-5 nūṟṟāṇṭil vāḻnta kāraikkālammaiyārum poykaiyār, pēyār, pūtattār ākiya mutaṉ mūṉṟu āḻvārkaḷum paktippāṭalkaḷaip pāṭiṉar. ki.pi. 6-9 nūṟṟāṇṭukaḷil caiva-vaiṇava pakti ilakkiyam perum aḷavil vaḷarntatu. pakti tōṉṟiyatu tamiḻakattil (“tirāviṭattil”) eṉa patmapurāṇamum pākavata makātmiyamum kuṟippiṭukiṉṟaṉa. pākavatam kūṟuvatu

utpannā tirāviṭē sāham vrttim karnāṭakē katā kvaciṉ kvaciṉ makārāṣṭrē kūrjarē jirṇatām katā
		
	(nāṉ piṟantatu tirāviṭattil; vaḷarntatu karnāṭakattil; mahārāṣṭirattil eppaṭiyō nilaittēṉ; kūrjarattil eṉ valimai kuṟaintatu)

pakti iyakkam ōraḷavukku maṉitam cārntatākavum, kīḻc cātiyiṉaruṭpaṭa aṉaivariṭamum potumaiyaiyum oṟṟumaiyaiyum vaḷarppatākavum amaintatu. ki.pi. 12ām nūṟṟāṇṭukkup piṟaku avviyakkam vaṭanāṭṭukkum paraviya poḻutu mukamatiyap parappāḷarkaḷiṉ tākkutaliliruntu intu matattaip pātukāttiṭa ōraḷavukku utaviyatu.

10. ulakac cevvilakkiyaṅkaḷil tamiḻc cevvilakkiyaṅ kaḷāṉa caṅka kāla ilakkiyaṅkaḷ taṉittaṉmaiyāṉa ciṟappu vāyntavai eṉpatai mēlē kaṇṭōm. ulaka moḻikaḷiṉ pakti ilakkiyaṅkaḷ aṉaittilum ki.pi. 400-900 cārnta tamiḻ pakti ilakkiyaṅkaḷ taṉic ciṟappu vāyntavai eṉpataiyum nallaṟiñar ēṟṟuḷḷaṉar.

11. tamiḻakattaip poṟuttavarai caṅka kāla camutāya - ilakkiyap pārvaiyiliruntu ki.pi. 6ām nūṟṟāṇṭukkup pintaiya pakti ilakkiya kāla camutāya ilakkiyap pārvai māṟupaṭṭatāka vaḷarntatu eṉpatai piṉvarumāṟu cuvelapil kūṟukiṟār. (Tamil Traditions on Subrahmanya - Murugan; 1991) 

“tamiḻakattil toṭakka kālap pakti iyakkam meyyāṉa ārvamum uyirum uḷḷatāka iruntatu; nāyaṉmārkaḷ āḻvārkaḷuḷ ciṟanta cilar uṇarvupūrvamāṉa uṉṉata paktip pāṭalkaḷai āyirak kaṇakkil pāṭiṉar. āyiṉum nāḷaṭaivil atu paṭippaṭiyāka uḷḷīṭiṉṟit taram tāḻa ārampittatu. uṇarvaṟṟa coṟkaṭṭukaḷ, kūṟiyatu kūṟal; carakkiṉmai, nakalkaḷ pōla amainta ērāḷamāṉa pāṭṭukaḷ; kuyuktiyāṉa, kiṟukkut taṉamāṉa kaṟpaṉaikaḷ; aruvarukkattakka vakaiyil mikaiyāṉa uṇarcci veḷippāṭukaḷ; uṇmaikkut toṭarpillāta kāṭci varuṇaṉaikaḷ; ivaiyellām nāḷaṭaivil paktip pāṭalkaḷil atikarittuk koṇṭē vantu kaṭaiciyil nam kālattil ‘pakti’ eṉpatu iṉṉum taram tāḻntatāka ākiviṭṭatu. kavitai, putiṉam, ciṟukatai, tiraip paṭam, pāṭalkaḷ, nāṭakaṅkaḷ, ciṟpakkalai mutaliya aṉaittut tuṟaikaḷilum. pāmarattaṉamāṉa kaḻicaṭai ciṉimākkaḷē (kāṭṭuttaṉamāṉa oliperukkik kūccalkaḷ; nayamaṟṟa miṉ viḷakku alaṅkāraṅkaḷ ākiyavaṟṟuṭaṉ) paktikku uruvam taruvaṉavāka amaintuviṭṭaṉa”

“maṉa nilaiyaip poṟutta varaiyilum cari, aṟivu pūrvamākavum cari paktiyiṉ ittakaiya pariṇāma vaḷarcciyum ataṉ viḷaivukaḷum tamiḻar paṇpāṭṭukkuk kēṭāka amaintu viṭṭaṉa eṉṟu karutāmalirukka iyalavillai. piṟa pakutikaḷ, nāṭukaḷiṉ paṇpāṭukaḷil pakti eṉpatu pala kūṟukaḷil oṉṟāka maṭṭumē amaintatu. māṟākat tamiḻakattilō ki.pi. 700/900liruntu 1900 varai pakti, pakti, paktiyait tavira vēṟu etuvum kuṟippiṭattakkatāka illai. ilakkiyattil maṭṭum (potuvāka pulavarulakam ēṟṟa ilakkiyaṅkaḷ varicaiyil cērkkappaṭāmal otukki vaittavaiyāṉa) nāṭṭup pāṭalkaḷ, kātal pāṭalkaḷ, paḷḷukaḷ, kuṟavañcikaḷ cila, ākiyavai paktiyallāta poruṇmai cārnta cila paṭaippukaḷāka uruvāyiṉa.”

12. intiyā muḻuvatum camayattilum, meyyiyalilum, peruntākkam viḷaivitta āticaṅkarācāriyār (788-820) aṉṟaiya tamiḻakattiṉ mēlaik kaṭaṟpakutiyil (iṉṟaiya kēraḷattil) kālaṭi eṉṉum ūril piṟanta pirāmaṇar āvār; atvaitak koḷkaiyiṉar. avar karunāṭakattil maicūrilum, kattiyavāril tuvārakaiyilum, oricāvil pūriyilum imaya malaiccāralil patrināttilum niṟuviya nāṉku maṭaṅkaḷ avarukkup piṉṉar akkoḷkaiyaip parappiṉa. vijayanakara āṭcik kālattil kumpakōṇattil niṟuvappaṭṭup piṉṉar kāñcipurattukku māṟṟappaṭṭa caṅkaramaṭam mutaṉ mutalil ciruṅkēri maṭattiṉ kiḷaiyākat tōṉṟiyatē eṉpatu pala aṟiñarkaḷiṉ karuttu ākum. (ār. kiruṣṇacāmi aiyar (1959) srī kumpakōṇa maṭam - oru vimarcaṉam . jē.vi. rāja kōpāla carmā 1963 jakatkuru caṅkara maṭa vimarsa (intiyil); 1987: Kanchi Kamakoti math, a myth; ār. kiruṣṇacāmi aiyar & kē. ār. vēṅkaṭarāmaṉ 1965The truth about the Kumbhakonam mutt) 

13. cōḻa nāṭṭiṉ perumpakuti ki.pi. 7-9 nūṟṟāṇṭukaḷil pallavar āṭciyiṉ kīḻ iruntatu. teṟkil pāṇṭiya nāṭṭai oṭṭiyirunta cōḻa nāṭṭiṉ ciṟupakutiyō pāṇṭiyar āṭciyiṉ kīḻ iruntatu. eṉavē cōḻanāṭṭaip paṟṟit taṉiyāka ivviyalil kuṟippiṭavillai.

14. caṅka kālattiṟku piṉṉar cēranāṭu citaṟuṇṭu palaciṟu aracarkaḷiṉ āṭciyiṉ kīḻ vantatu. acciṟṟaracukaḷ cila kālaṅkaḷil cutantaramākavum cila kālaṅkaḷil pāṇṭiya, cōḻa maṉṉarkaḷaip pēraracarāka ēṟṟum ceyalpaṭṭuvantaṉa. paṇṭaiya cēra nāṭṭiṉ kuṟippiṭattakka pakutiyai āṇṭa kaṭaicic cēra maṉṉar cēramāṉ perumāṉ (nāyaṉār). avar cuntarar naṇpaṉ ki.pi. 825il avar taṉ nāṭṭaittaṉ uṟaviṉarkaḷiṭamum ciṟṟaracarkaḷiṭamum pirittuk koṭuttu viṭṭuc cuntararuṭaṉ kailācattiṟkup pōyviṭṭatākak katai uḷḷatu. innikaḻcciyiliruntu (ki.pi. 825) toṭaṅkiyatē kollam āṇṭu eṉak karutukiṉṟaṉar.

15. ikkāla kaṭṭattil tamiḻnāṭṭiṉ vaṭapakutiyai āṇṭuvanta pallavarkaḷiṉ tōṟṟattaip paṟṟiya vēṟupaṭṭa karuttukaḷai muṉ iyalil kaṇṭōm. nīlakaṇṭa cāttiri tarum pallavar koṭivaḻi varumāṟu:
